note that the first two components
\textup{A}_{\textup{st},i}
of the vector potential
\mathbf{A}_{\textup{st}}
solve (
4.3
) under the 0-Dirichlet boundary conditions (
4.3
). Then by taking the odd extension of the Green function
G(x,y)=\frac{1}{|x-y|}
for the Poisson equation along
x_{3}=0,
we can define
\mathfrak{G}_{\textup{odd}}(x,y)=\frac{1}{|x-y|}-\frac{1}{|x-\bar{y}|}
and have

\textup{A}_{\textup{st},i}(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\mathfrak{G}_{\textup{odd}}(x,y)J_{\textup{st},i}(y)\,dy,

with
\bar{y}=(y_{1},y_{2},-y_{3})^{\top}.
On the other hand, since the third component
\textup{A}_{\textup{st},3}
satisfies the 0-Neumann boundary condition
\partial_{x_{3}}\textup{A}_{\textup{st},i}|_{x_{3}=0}=0,
on the boundary
x_{3}=0,
we take the even extension of the Green function and can define
\mathfrak{G}_{\textup{even}}(x,y)=\frac{1}{|x-y|}+\frac{1}{|x-\bar{y}|}
to obtain

\textup{A}_{\textup{st},3}(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\mathfrak{G}_{\textup{even}}(x,y)J_{\textup{st},3}(y)\,dy.

Since
\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}=\nabla\times\mathbf{A}_{\textup{st}},
we obtain that

(4.4)

\begin{split}\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st},i}(x)&=(-1)^{i}(\partial_{x_{3}}\textup{A}_{\textup{st},j}-\partial_{x_{j}}\textup{A}_{\textup{st},3})(x),\text{ for }i,j=1,2\text{ with }j\neq i,\\
&=(-1)^{i}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\partial_{x_{3}}\mathfrak{G}_{\textup{odd}}(x,y)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\hat{v}_{+,j}F_{+,\textup{st}}(y,v)-\hat{v}_{-,j}F_{-,\textup{st}}(y,v))dv\,dy\\
&\qquad-(-1)^{i}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\partial_{x_{j}}\mathfrak{G}_{\textup{even}}(x,y)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\hat{v}_{+,3}F_{+,\textup{st}}(y,v)-\hat{v}_{-,3}F_{-,\textup{st}}(y,v))dv\,dy\\
\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st},3}(x)&=(\partial_{x_{1}}\textup{A}_{\textup{st},2}-\partial_{x_{2}}\textup{A}_{\textup{st},1})(x)\\
&=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\partial_{x_{1}}\mathfrak{G}_{\textup{odd}}(x,y)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\hat{v}_{+,2}F_{+,\textup{st}}(y,v)-\hat{v}_{-,2}F_{-,\textup{st}}(y,v))dv\,dy\\
&\qquad-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\partial_{x_{2}}\mathfrak{G}_{\textup{odd}}(x,y)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\hat{v}_{+,1}F_{+,\textup{st}}(y,v)-\hat{v}_{-,1}F_{-,\textup{st}}(y,v))dv\,dy.\end{split}

Remark 4.4
.

Note that (
4.4
) satisfies
-\Delta\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}=\nabla\times J_{\textup{st}}
,
\partial_{x_{3}}\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st},i}(x_{\parallel},0)=(-1)^{j}4\pi J_{j}
for
i,j=1,2
with
j\neq i
, and
\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st},3}(x_{\parallel},0)=0
in the distributional sense.

4.1.2.
Solution Representations of
\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}
and its Potential
\phi_{\textup{st}}

Since
\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}
solves (
4.7
)
2
, there is a potential
\phi_{\textup{st}}
such that
\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}=-\nabla_{x}\phi_{\textup{st}}.
By (
4.7
)
3
, we obtain that

-\Delta\phi_{\textup{st}}=4\pi\rho_{\textup{st}},

for
x_{3}\geq 0
. We consider the perfect conductor boundary condition and assume that
\phi=0
on
x_{3}=0.
Then taking the odd extension of the Green function, we have

\phi_{\textup{st}}(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\mathfrak{G}_{\textup{odd}}(x,y)\rho_{\textup{st}}(y)\,dy,

with
\bar{y}=(y_{1},y_{2},-y_{3})^{\top}.
Then by taking the derivative in
x
, we obtain that

(4.5)

\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}=-\nabla_{x}\phi_{\textup{st}}=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\nabla_{x}\mathfrak{G}_{\textup{odd}}(x,y)\rho_{\textup{st}}(y)\,dy.

Remark 4.5
.

Note that (
4.5
) gives
\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st},i}(x_{\parallel},0)=0,
for
i=1,2,
and
\partial_{x_{3}}\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st},3}(x_{\parallel},0)=4\pi\rho_{\textup{st}}
in the distributional sense.

4.2.
Bootstrap Argument and Uniform
L^{\infty}
Estimates

For the nonlinear problem (
2.4
), we consider the sequence of iterated solutions
(F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{l},\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{l},\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{l})
for any
l\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}.
Construct the sequence
(F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{l},\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{l},\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{l})
via the solutions to the following stationary system

(4.6)

\begin{split}&\hat{v}_{\pm}\cdot\nabla_{x}F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{l+1}\pm\left(\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{l}+(\hat{v}_{\pm})\times\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{l}\mp m_{\pm}g\hat{e}_{3}\right)\cdot\nabla_{v}F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{l+1}=0,\\
&F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{l+1}(x_{\parallel},0,v)|_{v_{3}>0}=G_{\pm}(x_{\parallel},v),\end{split}

and the stationary Maxwell system

(4.7)

\begin{split}\nabla_{x}\times\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{l}=4\pi J^{l}_{\textup{st}},\ \nabla_{x}\times\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{l}=0,\ \ \nabla_{x}\cdot\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{l}=4\pi\rho^{l}_{\textup{st}},\ \nabla_{x}\cdot\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{l}=0,\end{split}

where we define

\rho^{l}_{\textup{st}}(x)\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(F^{l}_{+,\textup{st}}(x,v)-F^{l}_{+,\textup{st}}(x,v))dv\textup{ and }J^{l}_{\textup{st}}(x)\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\hat{v}_{+}F^{l}_{+,\textup{st}}(x,v)-\hat{v}_{-}F^{l}_{+,\textup{st}}(x,v))dv,

and we assume that
F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{0},\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{0},\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{0}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}0
. Recall that the boundary profiles
G_{\pm}
satisfy the assumption (
2.10
).

We consider the iterated stationary characteristic trajectory variables

Z_{\pm}^{l+1}(s;x,v)=(X_{\pm}^{l+1}(s;x,v),\allowbreak V_{\pm}^{l+1}(s;x,v))
which solve

(4.8)

\begin{split}\frac{dX_{\pm}^{l+1}(s)}{ds}&=\hat{V}_{\pm}^{l+1}(s)=\frac{V_{\pm}^{l+1}(s)}{\sqrt{m_{\pm}^{2}+|V_{\pm}^{l+1}(s)|^{2}}},\\
\frac{dV_{\pm}^{l+1}(s)}{ds}&=\pm\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{l}(s,X_{\pm}^{l+1}(s))\pm\hat{V}_{\pm}^{l+1}(s)\times\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{l}(s,X_{\pm}^{l+1}(s))-m_{\pm}g\hat{e}_{3},\end{split}

where
\hat{e}_{3}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}(0,0,1)^{\top}
and
\hat{v}_{\pm}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}\frac{v}{v^{0}_{\pm}}=\frac{v}{\sqrt{m_{\pm}^{2}+|v|^{2}}}
. Iterating the stationary characteristic trajectory (
3.45
), we define

(4.9)

\begin{split}&t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{f}}(x,v)=\sup\{s\in[0,\infty):(X_{\pm,\textup{st}})_{3}^{l+1}(\tau;x,v)>0\ \textup{ for all }\tau\in(0,s)\}\geq 0,\\
&t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}(x,v)=\sup\{s\in[0,\infty):(X_{\pm,\textup{st}})_{3}^{l+1}(-\tau;x,v)>0\ \textup{ for all }\tau\in(0,s)\}\geq 0\\
&x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1}(x,v)=X_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{l+1}\left(-t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}(x,v);x,v\right)\in\partial\Omega,\\
&v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1}(x,v)=V_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{l+1}\left(-t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}(x,v);x,v\right).\end{split}

As in the solution in the mild form (
3.43
) for the dynamical case, we can also write our solution
F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{l+1}
in the steady case as

(4.10)

F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{l+1}(x,v)=G_{\pm}((X_{\pm,\textup{st}})_{\parallel}^{l+1}(-t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}};x,v),V_{\pm}^{l+1}(-t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}};x,v)).

Now we obtain the following uniform
L^{\infty}
estimates for the iterated sequence
(F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{k},\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{k},\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{k})
with
k\in\mathbb{N}
via bootstrap argument:

Proposition 4.6
.

For any
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

(4.11)

\|e^{\frac{\beta}{2}|x_{\parallel}|}e^{\frac{\beta}{2}v^{0}_{\pm}}e^{\frac{1}{2}m_{\pm}g\beta x_{3}}F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{k}(\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C,\textup{ and }|\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{k}(x)|,\ |\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{k}(x)|\leq\min\{m_{+},m_{-}\}\frac{g}{16}\frac{1}{\langle x\rangle^{2}},

for some
C>0
with
\min\{m_{+},m_{-}\}g\geq 8
and
\beta>1
.

It is trivial that the solutions are zero and satisfy (
4.11
) when
k=0.
Assume (
4.11
) holds for
k=l.
Then we prove that the next sequence element
(F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{l+1},\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{l+1},\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{l+1})
will satisfy the same upper-bounds (
4.11
).

4.2.1.
Weighted
L^{\infty}
Estimate for the Velocity Distribution
F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{l+1}

Using (
4.10
), we observe that

(4.12)

|F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{l+1}(x,v)|=|G_{\pm}((X_{\pm,\textup{st}})_{\parallel}^{l+1}(-t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}};x,v),V_{\pm}^{l+1}(-t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}};x,v))|\\
=\frac{1}{\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}(Z_{\pm}^{l+1}(-t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}};x,v))}\|(\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}G_{\pm})((X_{\pm,\textup{st}})_{\parallel}^{l+1}(-t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}};x,v),V_{\pm}^{l+1}(-t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}};x,v))\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}(\gamma_{-})}.

Using the boundary condition (
2.10
) and the weight comparison (
3.53
), we have

(4.13)

|F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{l+1}(x,v)|\leq Ce^{-\frac{1}{2}\beta v^{0}_{\pm}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}m_{\pm}g\beta x_{3}}e^{-\frac{\beta}{2}|x_{\parallel}|},

where the weight function
\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}
is defined in (
3.46
). This proves the bootstrap assumption (
4.11
) for
F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{l+1}
.

4.2.2.
L^{\infty}
Estimates for the Steady Fields
\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{l+1}
and
\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{l+1}

Now, given the estimates (
4.13
) for the steady distribution
F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{l+1}
, we will prove the bootstrap estimates (
4.11
) for the fields
\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{l+1}
and
\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{l+1}
using the field representations (
4.4
) and (
4.5
).

For
i=1,2,3
, the field components
\mathbf{E}^{l+1}_{\textup{st},i}
of
\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{l+1}
in (
4.5
) solving (
4.7
) satisfy that

|\mathbf{E}^{l+1}_{\textup{st},i}(x)|\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\left|\partial_{x_{i}}\mathfrak{G}_{\textup{odd}}(x,y)\right|\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}F^{l+1}_{+,\textup{st}}(y,v)dv-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}F^{l+1}_{-,\textup{st}}(y,v)dv\right|\ dy.

Using the estimate (
4.13
), we observe that

(4.14)

|\mathbf{E}^{l+1}_{\textup{st},i}(x)|\leq\sum_{\pm}2C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}dy\ \left|\partial_{x_{i}}\mathfrak{G}_{\textup{odd}}(x,y)\right|e^{-\frac{\beta}{2}|y_{\parallel}|}e^{-\frac{1}{2}m_{\pm}g\beta y_{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dv\ e^{-\frac{1}{2}\beta v^{0}_{\pm}}\\
\lesssim\sum_{\pm}2\frac{C}{\beta^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}dy\ \left|\partial_{x_{i}}\mathfrak{G}_{\textup{odd}}(x,y)\right|e^{-\frac{\beta}{2}|y_{\parallel}|}e^{-\frac{1}{2}m_{\pm}g\beta y_{3}},

where we further used that

(4.15)

\begin{split}\int_{{\mathbb{R}^{3}}}dv\ e^{-\frac{\beta}{2}v^{0}_{\pm}}&=\int_{{\mathbb{R}^{3}}}dv\ e^{-\frac{\beta}{2}\sqrt{m_{\pm}^{2}+|v|^{2}}}=4\pi\int_{0}^{\infty}d|v|\ |v|^{2}e^{-\frac{\beta}{2}\sqrt{m_{\pm}^{2}+|v|^{2}}}\\
&=4\pi\int_{m_{\pm}}^{\infty}dz\ z\sqrt{z^{2}-m^{2}_{\pm}}e^{-\frac{\beta}{2}z}\leq 4\pi\int_{0}^{\infty}dz\ z^{2}e^{-\frac{\beta}{2}z}=\frac{32\pi}{\beta^{3}}\int_{0}^{\infty}dz^{\prime}\ z^{\prime 2}e^{-z^{\prime}}\approx\frac{1}{\beta^{3}},\end{split}

where we made the changes of variables
|v|\mapsto z\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}\sqrt{m_{\pm}^{2}+|v|^{2}}
and
z\mapsto z^{\prime}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}\frac{\beta}{2}z.

Then since
\left|\partial_{x_{i}}\mathfrak{G}_{\textup{odd}}(x,y)\right|\leq\frac{1}{|x-y|^{2}}+\frac{1}{|\bar{x}-y|^{2}}
and the upper bound is even in
y_{3}
, note that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}dy\ \left|\partial_{x_{i}}\mathfrak{G}_{\textup{odd}}(x,y)\right|e^{-\frac{\beta}{2}|y_{\parallel}|}e^{-\frac{1}{2}m_{\pm}g\beta y_{3}}\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dy\ \left(\frac{1}{|x-y|^{2}}+\frac{1}{|\bar{x}-y|^{2}}\right)e^{-\frac{\beta}{2}|y_{\parallel}|}e^{-\frac{1}{2}m_{\pm}g\beta|y_{3}|}\\
\lesssim\frac{1}{m_{\pm}g\beta^{3}}\frac{1}{\langle x\rangle^{2}},

using the elementary inequality

(4.16)

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dz\ \frac{e^{-a|z_{\parallel}|}e^{-b|z_{3}|}}{|x_{\parallel}-z_{\parallel}|^{k}+|x_{3}-z_{3}|^{k}}\lesssim\frac{1}{a^{2}b}\frac{1}{\langle x\rangle^{k}},

for
k<3.

Therefore, in (
4.14
),
choosing
\min\{m_{+},m_{-}\}g\beta^{3}\gg 1,
we have

(4.17)

|\mathbf{E}^{l+1}_{\textup{st},i}(x)|\lesssim\frac{1}{\min\{m_{+},m_{-}\}g\beta^{6}}\frac{1}{\langle x\rangle^{2}}\ll\min\{m_{+},m_{-}\}g\frac{1}{\langle x\rangle^{2}}.

Moreover, since
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\hat{v}_{+,i}||F_{+,\textup{st}}(y,v)|dv\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|F_{+,\textup{st}}(y,v)|dv,

\mathbf{B}^{l+1}_{\textup{st}}(x)
in (
4.4
) also has the same upper-bound (up to constant) as that of
\mathbf{E}^{l+1}_{\textup{st}}(x)
and hence

|\mathbf{B}^{l+1}_{\textup{st}}(x)|\ll\min\{m_{+},m_{-}\}g\frac{1}{\langle x\rangle^{2}}.

Altogether, we have

(4.18)

|\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{l+1}(x)|,\ |\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{l+1}(x)|\leq\min\{m_{+},m_{-}\}\frac{g}{16}\frac{1}{\langle x\rangle^{2}},

which closes the bootstrap argument by proving the upper-bounds in (
4.11
) at the sequential level of
(l+1).

Proof of Proposition
4.6
.

Proposition
4.6
now follows by (
4.13
) and (
4.18
).
∎

4.3.
Derivative Estimates

We can further show that the stationary solution satisfies the following regularity estimates at the sequential level. We first define the following kinetic weight functions:

Definition 4.7
.

(4.19)

\tilde{\alpha}_{\pm,\textup{st}}(x,v)\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}\sqrt{\frac{\alpha_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{2}(x,v)}{1+\alpha_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{2}(x,v)}},

where
\alpha_{\pm,\textup{st}}
is defined as

(4.20)

\alpha_{\pm,\textup{st}}(x,v)=\sqrt{x_{3}^{2}+\left|(\hat{v}_{\pm})_{3}\right|^{2}-2\left((\mathscr{F}^{l}_{\pm})_{3}(x_{\parallel},0,v)\right)\frac{x_{3}}{(v^{0}_{\pm})}}.

with
(\mathscr{F}^{l}_{\pm})_{\textup{st}}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}\pm\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{l}\pm\hat{v}_{\pm}\times\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{l}-m_{\pm}g\hat{e}_{3}.

Then we have the following derivative estimates associated to the kinetic weight
\tilde{\alpha}_{\pm,\textup{st}}
:

Proposition 4.8
.

Fix
m>4
and
R>0.

Suppose that the boundary data
G_{\pm}
satisfy

(4.21)

\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}G_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x_{\parallel},v}}+\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\nabla_{v}G_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x_{\parallel},v}}<\infty.

Consider the corresponding solution sequence
(F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{l},\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{l},\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{l})_{l\in\mathbb{N}}
of (
4.6
)–(
4.8
) associated to the boundary data
G_{\pm}
. Fix any arbitrary
l\in\mathbb{N}
. Define

(4.22)

(\mathscr{F}^{l}_{\pm})_{\textup{st}}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}\pm\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{l}\pm\hat{v}_{\pm}\times\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{l}-m_{\pm}g\hat{e}_{3}.

Suppose that

(4.23)

\|\nabla_{x}(\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{l},\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{l})\|_{L^{\infty}}<C_{1}\text{ and }\|(\mathscr{F}^{l}_{\pm})_{\textup{st}}\|_{L^{\infty}}<C_{2},

for some
C_{1}>0
and
C_{2}>0.

Define
\Omega_{R}={\mathbb{R}^{3}}\times[0,R].
Then

(4.24)

\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{l+1}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{R}\times{\mathbb{R}^{3}})}+\left\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\tilde{\alpha}_{\pm,\textup{st}}\partial_{x_{3}}F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{l+1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{R}\times{\mathbb{R}^{3}})}+\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\nabla_{v}F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{l+1}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{R}\times{\mathbb{R}^{3}})}\leq C_{R},

for some constant
C_{R}>0
which depends only on
R
,
C_{1},C_{2}
and
G_{\pm}
.
Suppose that
-(\mathscr{F}^{l}_{\pm})_{\textup{st},3}(x_{\parallel},0,v)>c_{0},
for some
c_{0}>0.
Moreover, the following estimates hold:

(4.25)

\|(\mathbf{E}^{l+1}_{\textup{st}},\mathbf{B}^{l+1}_{\textup{st}})\|_{W^{1,\infty}_{x}(\Omega)}\lesssim\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}F^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}(\Omega\times{\mathbb{R}^{3}})}\\
+\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}F^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}(\Omega\times{\mathbb{R}^{3}})}+\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\tilde{\alpha}_{\pm,\textup{st}}(x,v)\partial_{x_{3}}F^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}(\Omega\times{\mathbb{R}^{3}})},

where the weight
\tilde{\alpha}_{\pm,\textup{st}}
is defined as in (
4.19
).

Remark 4.9
.

The constant
C_{R}
remains finite on each finite slab
x_{3}\in[0,R]
.
Once the estimates are established on
[0,R]
, they can be extended to
[R,2R]
by redefining the inflow boundary data at
x_{3}=R
using the solution values there. Note that this new inflow data also satisfy (
4.21
) by (
4.24
). Iterating this continuation procedure covers all intervals
[kR,(k+1)R]
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
, and thus yields the desired global regularity estimates on
x_{3}\in[0,\infty)
.

We note that the derivative estimate for the distribution (
4.24
) is uniform in
l
and hence the derivative estimate for the fields (
4.25
) is also uniform in
l
by (
4.13
). Hence those bounds are even preserved when we pass to the limit
l\to\infty.

For the proof of the proposition, we collect several lemmas on the kinetic weight
\tilde{\alpha}_{\pm,\textup{st}}
including the velocity lemma (Lemma
4.10
) originally established by Guo
[
13
]
.

Lemma 4.10

(Velocity Lemma)
.

Let
\alpha_{\pm,\textup{st}}
and
\tilde{\alpha}_{\pm,\textup{st}}
be defined as in (
4.20
) and (
4.19
), respectively. Define
(\mathscr{F}^{l}_{\pm})_{\textup{st}}
as (
4.22
). Suppose

\|\mathbf{E}^{l}_{\textup{st}}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\mathbf{B}^{l}_{\textup{st}}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla_{x}(\mathscr{F}^{l}_{\pm})_{\textup{st}}\|_{L^{\infty}}<C.

Suppose that for all
x_{\parallel}\in\mathbb{R}^{2},

-(\mathscr{F}^{l}_{\pm})_{3,\textup{st}}(x_{\parallel},0)>c_{0},
for some
c_{0}>0.
Then for any
(x,v)\in\Omega\times{\mathbb{R}^{3}},
with the trajectory
X_{\pm}^{l+1}(s;x,v)
and
V_{\pm}^{l+1}(s;x,v)
satisfying (
4.8
),

(4.26)

e^{-10\frac{C}{c_{0}}|s|}\tilde{\alpha}_{\pm,\textup{st}}(x,v)\leq\tilde{\alpha}_{\pm,\textup{st}}(s,X_{\pm}^{l+1}(s;x,v),V_{\pm}^{l+1}(s;x,v))\leq e^{10\frac{C}{c_{0}}|s|}\tilde{\alpha}_{\pm,\textup{st}}(x,v)

In addition, regarding the stationary material derivative

\frac{D}{Ds}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}(\hat{V}_{\pm}(s))\cdot\nabla_{x}+(\mathscr{F}^{l}_{\pm})_{\textup{st}}(X^{l}_{\pm}(s))\cdot\nabla_{v},
we have

(4.27)

\left|\frac{D}{Ds}\alpha_{\pm}^{2}(s)\right|\leq 20\frac{C}{c_{0}}\alpha_{\pm}^{2}(s).

Proof.

We first observe that

\frac{D}{Ds}\tilde{\alpha}_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{2}=\frac{1}{1+\alpha_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{2}}\frac{D}{Ds}\alpha_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{2}-\frac{\alpha_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{2}}{(1+\alpha_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{2})^{2}}\frac{D}{Ds}\alpha_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{2}=\frac{1}{(1+\alpha_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{2})^{2}}\frac{D}{Ds}\alpha_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{2}.

Then using the bound (
4.27
) of the material derivative
\frac{D}{Ds}\alpha_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{2}
we further obtain

\frac{D}{Ds}\tilde{\alpha}_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{2}\leq 20\frac{C}{c_{0}(1+\alpha_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{2})}\frac{\alpha_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{2}}{1+\alpha_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{2}}\leq 20\frac{C}{c_{0}}\tilde{\alpha}_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{2}.

By the Grönwall lemma, we finally obtain

\tilde{\alpha}_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{2}(s,X_{\pm}^{l+1}(s),V_{\pm}^{l+1}(s))\leq e^{\frac{20C}{c_{0}}|s|}\tilde{\alpha}_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{2}(x,v).

This completes the proof of Lemma
4.10
. Lastly, the proof of (
4.27
) follows by
[
5
, Eq. (4.10)]
.
∎

We also record the following upper bound on the singularity
\frac{1}{|\hat{V}^{l+1}_{\pm,3}|}
:

Lemma 4.11

(Lemma 10 of
[
5
]
)
.

For
(x,v)\in\Omega\times{\mathbb{R}^{3}},
let the trajectory
X_{\pm}^{l+1}(s;x,v)
and
V_{\pm}^{l+1}(s;x,v)
satisfy (
4.8
).
Suppose for all
x,v
,
-(\mathscr{F}^{l}_{\pm})_{3,\textup{st}}(x_{\parallel},0,v)>c_{0},
then there exists a constant
C
depending on
g,

\|\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{l}\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\Omega)},
and
\|\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{l}\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\Omega)},
such that

(4.28)

\frac{t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}(x,v)}{(\hat{V}_{\pm}^{l+1})_{3}(-t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}})}\leq\frac{C}{c_{0}}\max_{s\in\{-t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}},0\}}\sqrt{m_{\pm}^{2}+|V_{\pm}^{l+1}(s)|^{2}}.

Proof of Proposition
4.8
.

Fix
m>4
. By differentiating the stationary Vlasov equation (
4.6
) with respect to
x_{\parallel}
, we observe that
(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}|\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{l+1}|
is bounded from above by

\displaystyle(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}|\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{l+1}(x,v)|

\displaystyle\leq(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\bigg|(\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}G_{\pm})((x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel},v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})\cdot\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}(x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel}+(\nabla_{v}G_{\pm})((x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel},v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})\cdot\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1}\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}|(\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}G_{\pm})((x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel},v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})||\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}(x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel}|+(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}|(\nabla_{v}G_{\pm})((x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel},v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})||\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1}|.

In general, note that for
-t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}\leq s\leq t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{f}},

(4.29)

(v^{0}_{\pm})\lesssim\langle V_{\pm}^{l+1}(s)\rangle+\bigg|\int_{s}^{0}d\tau\ (\mathscr{F}^{l}_{\pm})_{\textup{st}}(\mathscr{X}^{l+1}_{\pm}(\tau),V_{\pm}^{l+1}(\tau))\bigg|\lesssim\langle V_{\pm}^{l+1}(s)\rangle+C_{2}|s|,

by (
4.23
). Also recall (
3.51
) that we have for
x_{3}\in[0,R],

(4.30)

t_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}+t_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{f}}\lesssim C_{2}(\sqrt{m_{\pm}^{2}+|v_{\pm}|^{2}}+m_{\pm}gx_{3})\lesssim v^{0}_{\pm}+R,

under (
4.23
).
On the other hand, given (
4.23
), note that the derivatives of
x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1}
and
v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1}
satisfy the same upper-bounds estimates (
6.10
) with the dynamical trajectory variables
\mathscr{Z}_{\pm}^{l+1}=(\mathscr{X}^{l+1}_{\pm},\mathscr{V}^{l+1}_{\pm})
and the variables
(t,x,v)
now replaced by the stationary variables
Z^{l+1}=(X^{l+1}_{\pm},V^{l+1}_{\pm})
and
(0,x,v)
, respectively. Thus, using the stationary counterparts of (
6.10
)–(
6.11
), we obtain

(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}|\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{l+1}(x,v)|\lesssim C\bigg((v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}|(\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}G_{\pm})((x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel},v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})|\bigg|\frac{t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}}{|\hat{V}_{\pm}^{l+1}(-t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}})|(v^{0}_{\pm})}+1\bigg|\\
+(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}|(\nabla_{v}G_{\pm})((x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel},v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})|\bigg|\frac{t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}}{|\hat{V}_{\pm}^{l+1}(-t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}})|(v^{0}_{\pm})}+1\bigg|\bigg).

By Lemma
4.11
and (
4.30
), we further observe that

(4.31)

\bigg|\frac{t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}}{|(\hat{V}_{\pm}^{l+1})_{3}(-t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}})|(v^{0}_{\pm})}\bigg|\leq\frac{C}{c_{0}}\frac{\max_{s\in\{-t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}},0\}}\sqrt{m_{\pm}^{2}+|V_{\pm}^{l+1}(s)|^{2}}}{(v^{0}_{\pm})}\\
\lesssim\frac{C}{c_{0}}\sup_{-t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}<s<0}\left(1+\frac{1}{(v^{0}_{\pm})}\bigg|\int_{s}^{0}(\mathscr{F}^{l}_{\pm})_{\textup{st}}(\mathscr{X}^{l+1}_{\pm}(\tau),V_{\pm}^{l+1}(\tau))d\tau\bigg|\right)\lesssim\frac{C}{c_{0}}\left(1+\frac{C_{2}t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}}{(v^{0}_{\pm})}\right)\lesssim C_{R}.

Thus we conclude that

\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{l+1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}(\Omega_{R}\times{\mathbb{R}^{3}})}\lesssim C_{R}\bigg(\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}G_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x_{\parallel},v}}+\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\nabla_{v}G_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x_{\parallel},v}}\bigg).

Regarding the derivative
\partial_{x_{3}}F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{l+1},
we differentiate the Vlasov equation (
4.6
) with respect to
x_{3}
and obtain

\displaystyle(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\tilde{\alpha}_{\pm,\textup{st}}(x,v)|\partial_{x_{3}}F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{l+1}(x,v)|

\displaystyle\leq(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\tilde{\alpha}_{\pm,\textup{st}}(x,v)\bigg|(\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}G_{\pm})((x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel},v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})\cdot\partial_{x_{3}}(x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel}+(\nabla_{v}G_{\pm})((x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel},v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})\cdot\partial_{x_{3}}v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1}\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\tilde{\alpha}_{\pm,\textup{st}}(x,v)|(\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}G_{\pm})((x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel},v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})||\partial_{x_{3}}(x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel}|

\displaystyle+(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\tilde{\alpha}_{\pm,\textup{st}}(x,v)|(\nabla_{v}G_{\pm})((x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel},v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})||\partial_{x_{3}}v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1}|.

Again, by the stationary counterpart of (
6.10
), we have

\displaystyle(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\tilde{\alpha}_{\pm,\textup{st}}(x,v)|(\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}G_{\pm})((x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel},v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})||\partial_{x_{3}}(x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel}|

\displaystyle+(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\tilde{\alpha}_{\pm,\textup{st}}(x,v)|(\nabla_{v}G_{\pm})((x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel},v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})||\partial_{x_{3}}v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1}|

\displaystyle\lesssim C\bigg((v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}|(\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}G_{\pm})((x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel},v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})|\tilde{\alpha}_{\pm,\textup{st}}(x,v)\left|\frac{1}{|(\hat{V}_{\pm}^{l+1})_{3}(-t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}})|}+\frac{1}{\langle v\rangle}\right|

\displaystyle+(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}|(\nabla_{v}G_{\pm})((x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel},v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})|\tilde{\alpha}_{\pm,\textup{st}}(x,v)\left|\frac{1}{|(\hat{V}_{\pm}^{l+1})_{3}(-t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}})|}+1\right|\bigg).

By using Lemma
4.10
, (
4.29
), and (
4.30
) with
s=-t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}
, we conclude that

\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\tilde{\alpha}_{\pm}\partial_{x_{3}}F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{l+1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}(\Omega_{R}\times{\mathbb{R}^{3}})}\lesssim C_{R}\bigg(\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}G_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x_{\parallel},v}}+\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\nabla_{v}G_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x_{\parallel},v}}\bigg).

Regarding the momentum derivative
|\nabla_{v}F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{l+1}|,
we differentiate (
4.6
) with respect to
v
and obtain

\displaystyle(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}|\nabla_{v}F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{l+1}(x,v)|

\displaystyle\leq(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\bigg|(\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}G_{\pm})((x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel},v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})\cdot\nabla_{v}(x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel}+(\nabla_{v}G_{\pm})((x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel},v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})\cdot\nabla_{v}v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1}\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}|(\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}G_{\pm})((x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel},v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})||\nabla_{v}(x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel}|+(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}|(\nabla_{v}G_{\pm})((x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel},v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})||\nabla_{v}v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1}|.

Using the stationary counterpart of (
6.10
), we obtain

(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}|\nabla_{v}F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{l+1}(x,v)|\lesssim C_{T}(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m-1}\bigg(|(\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}G_{\pm})((x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel},v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})|\frac{t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}}{|\hat{\mathscr{V}}_{\pm}^{l+1}(t-t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}})|}\\
+|(\nabla_{v}G_{\pm})((x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel},v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})|\bigg|\frac{t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}}{|\hat{\mathscr{V}}_{\pm}^{l+1}(t-t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}})|(v^{0}_{\pm})}+1\bigg|\bigg).

By using (
4.31
) and (
4.29
) with
s=-t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}
, we conclude that

\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\nabla_{v}F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{l+1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}(\Omega_{R}\times{\mathbb{R}^{3}})}\lesssim C_{R}\bigg(\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}G_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x_{\parallel},v}}+\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m-1}\nabla_{v}G_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x_{\parallel},v}}\bigg).

Lastly, concerning the derivatives of the stationary fields
\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}},\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}
, the arguments of Lemma
7.2
and Lemma
7.4
, stated in the dynamical case, extend to the stationary case with only minor modifications. For brevity, we omit the proof.

∎

4.3.1.
Enhanced Decay Estimates for
|\nabla_{v}F_{\pm,\textup{st}}|

In this subsection, we further obtain enhanced decay estimates for
|\nabla_{v}F_{\pm,\textup{st}}|
given that the incoming boundary profile
G_{\pm}
further satisfies the following fast-decay condition on the first-order derivative in the velocity variable.

Proposition 4.12

(Momentum Derivatives)
.

Suppose (
4.23
) holds. Suppose that
G_{\pm}
satisfy

(4.32)

\|\mathrm{w}^{2}_{\pm,\beta}(\cdot,0,\cdot)\nabla_{x_{\parallel},v}G_{\pm}(\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x_{\parallel},v}}<\infty.

Then for each
l\in\mathbb{N},
we have

(4.33)

\|\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}\nabla_{v}F^{l}_{\textup{st}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq C\|\mathrm{w}^{2}_{\pm,\beta}(\cdot,0,\cdot)\nabla_{x_{\parallel},v}G_{\pm}(\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x_{\parallel},v}},

for some
C>0
.

Note that (
4.33
) is uniform in
l
and is preserved when we pass to the limit
l\to\infty.

Proof for Proposition
4.12
.

Fix
l\in\mathbb{N}.
By Proposition
4.8
, we have for some
C_{1}>0
and
C_{2}>0
,

\|\nabla_{x}(\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{l},\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{l})\|_{L^{\infty}}<C_{1},\text{ and }\|(\mathscr{F}^{l}_{\pm})_{\textup{st}}\|_{L^{\infty}}<C_{2}.

By taking the momentum derivative on (
4.10
), we obtain

\nabla_{v}F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{l+1}(x,v)=(\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}G_{\pm})((x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel},v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})\cdot\nabla_{v}(x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel}+(\nabla_{v}G_{\pm})((x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel},v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})\cdot\nabla_{v}v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1},

where the stationary backward exit position and velocity
x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1}
and
v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1}
are defined as

(4.34)

\begin{split}x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1}(x,v)=X^{l+1}\left(-t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}(x,v);x,v\right)\in\partial\Omega,\ \ v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1}(x,v)=V^{l+1}\left(-t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}(x,v);x,v\right).\end{split}

Then, given (
4.23
), we note that the derivatives of
x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1}
and
v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1}
satisfy the same upper-bounds estimates (
6.10
) with the dynamical trajectory variables
\mathscr{Z}_{\pm}^{l+1}=(\mathscr{X}^{l+1}_{\pm},\mathscr{V}^{l+1}_{\pm})
the variables
(t,x,v)
now replaced by the stationary variables
Z^{l+1}=(X^{l+1}_{\pm},V^{l+1}_{\pm})
and
(0,x,v)
, respectively. Therefore, using (
6.10
), we observe that

\displaystyle|\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}\nabla_{v}F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{l+1}(x,v)|

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}(x,v)|(\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}G_{\pm})((x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel},v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})||\nabla_{v}(x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel}|

\displaystyle+\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}(x,v)|(\nabla_{v}G_{\pm})((x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel},v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})||\nabla_{v}v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}(x,v)\bigg(|(\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}G_{\pm})((x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel},v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})\left(\frac{t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}}{|(\hat{V}^{l+1}_{\pm})_{3}(-t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}})|(v^{0}_{\pm})}+\frac{t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}}{v^{0}_{\pm}}\right)

\displaystyle+|(\nabla_{v}G_{\pm})((x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel},v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})|\bigg|\frac{t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}}{|(\hat{V}^{l+1}_{\pm})_{3}(-t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}})|(v^{0}_{\pm})}+(v^{0}_{\pm})^{-1}\bigg|\bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}(x,v)}\left(\frac{\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}(x,v)}{\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}(x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1}(x,v),v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1}(x,v))}\right)^{2}\bigg(|(\mathrm{w}^{2}_{\pm,\beta}\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}G_{\pm})((x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel},v_{\pm,\mathbf{b}})|

\displaystyle+|(\mathrm{w}^{2}_{\pm,\beta}\nabla_{v}G_{\pm})((x_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel},v_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})|\bigg),

by Lemma
6.7
. Then we further use the weight comparison (
3.52
) and observe that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}(x,v)}

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}\left(Z^{l+1}_{\pm}\left(0;x,v\right)\right)}{\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}(Z^{l+1}_{\pm}(-t^{l+1}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}(x,v);x,v))}\right)^{2}\leq\frac{1}{\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}(x,v)}e^{\left(\left\|\mathbf{E}^{l}_{\textup{st}}\right\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}+1\right)\frac{64\beta}{13m_{\pm}g}(\sqrt{m_{\pm}^{2}+|v_{\pm}|^{2}}+m_{\pm}gx_{3})}

\displaystyle\leq e^{(\min\{m_{-},m_{+}\}g)\left(\frac{1}{16}+\frac{1}{8}\right)\frac{64\beta}{13m_{\pm}g}(\sqrt{m_{\pm}^{2}+|v_{\pm}|^{2}}+m_{\pm}gx_{3})}e^{-\beta v_{\pm}^{0}-m_{\pm}g\beta x_{3}-\frac{\beta}{2}|x_{\parallel}|}

\displaystyle\leq e^{\frac{12\beta}{13}(\sqrt{m_{\pm}^{2}+|v_{\pm}|^{2}}+m_{\pm}gx_{3})}e^{-\beta v_{\pm}^{0}-m_{\pm}g\beta x_{3}-\frac{\beta}{2}|x_{\parallel}|}\leq e^{-\frac{1}{13}\beta v_{\pm}^{0}-\frac{1}{13}m_{\pm}g\beta x_{3}-\frac{\beta}{2}|x_{\parallel}|}\leq 1,

given that
\mathbf{E}^{l}_{\textup{st}}
satisfies the upper-bound (
4.11
) and that

\min\{m_{+},m_{-}\}g\geq 8.
This completes the proof.∎

4.4.
Stability and Construction of Solutions

Given the uniform estimates for the iterated sequence elements of steady states
(F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{l},\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{l},\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{l})
and the enhanced decay estimates on the momentum derivatives
\nabla_{v}F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{l}
, we can now prove the stability of the sequence which yields Cauchy property of the sequences. Then we will obtain the strong convergence to the limit
(F_{\pm,\textup{st}},\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}},\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}})
. This is necessary to pass to the limit on the nonlinear terms. Fix
N_{0}\in\mathbb{N}.
Then for any
k,n\geq N_{0}
integers with
k\geq n
, we have

(4.35)

\begin{split}(F^{k}_{\pm,\textup{st}}-F^{n}_{\pm,\textup{st}})(x_{\parallel},0,v)|_{\gamma_{-}}=0,\end{split}

and

(\hat{v}_{\pm})\cdot\nabla_{x}(F^{k}_{\pm,\textup{st}}-F^{n}_{\pm,\textup{st}})+\left(\pm\mathbf{E}^{k-1}_{\textup{st}}\pm(\hat{v}_{\pm})\times\mathbf{B}^{k-1}_{\textup{st}}-m_{\pm}g\hat{e}_{3}\right)\cdot\nabla_{v}(F^{k}_{\pm,\textup{st}}-F^{n}_{\pm,\textup{st}})\\
=-\left(\pm(\mathbf{E}^{k-1}_{\textup{st}}-\mathbf{E}^{n-1}_{\textup{st}})\pm(\hat{v}_{\pm})\times(\mathbf{B}^{k-1}_{\textup{st}}-\mathbf{B}^{n-1}_{\textup{st}})\right)\cdot\nabla_{v}F^{n}_{\pm,\textup{st}},

by (
4.6
). By (
4.35
), we have

(4.36)

(F^{k}_{\pm,\textup{st}}-F^{n}_{\pm,\textup{st}})(x,v)=\mp\int^{0}_{-t^{k}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}}\left((\mathbf{E}^{k-1}-\mathbf{E}^{n-1})(X_{\pm}^{k}(s))+\hat{V}^{k}_{\pm}(s)\times(\mathbf{B}^{k-1}-\mathbf{B}^{n-1})(X_{\pm}^{k}(s))\right)\\
\cdot\nabla_{v}F^{n}_{\pm,\textup{st}}(X_{\pm}^{k}(s),V_{\pm}^{k}(s))ds,

using the iterated stationary characteristic trajectories
(X_{\pm}^{k},V_{\pm}^{k})
in (
4.8
).
Therefore, we have

(4.37)

|(F^{k}_{\pm,\textup{st}}-F^{n}_{\pm,\textup{st}})(x,v)|\\
\leq t^{k}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}\sup_{s\in[-t^{k}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}},0]}|(\nabla_{v}F^{n}_{\pm,\textup{st}})(X_{\pm,\textup{st}}(s),V_{\pm,\textup{st}}(s))|\left(\|(\mathbf{E}^{k-1}_{\textup{st}}-\mathbf{E}^{n-1}_{\textup{st}})(\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|(\mathbf{B}^{k-1}_{\textup{st}}-\mathbf{B}^{n-1}_{\textup{st}})(\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\right).

Indeed, given that
\|(\mathbf{E}^{k-1}_{\textup{st}},\mathbf{B}^{k-1}_{\textup{st}})\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\min\{m_{+},m_{-}\}\frac{g}{16}
holds by the previous uniform estimates, we have

t^{k}_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}\leq\frac{3}{m_{\pm}g}(v^{0}_{\pm}+m_{\pm}gx_{3})

by (
3.51
). By using the uniform estimate (
4.33
) on the momentum derivative
\nabla_{v}F^{n}_{\pm,\textup{st}}
and the weight comparison estimate (
3.53
), we have

(4.38)

e^{\frac{1}{2}\beta|x_{\parallel}|}e^{\frac{1}{4}\beta\left(v^{0}_{\pm}+m_{\pm}gx_{3}\right)}|(F^{k}_{\pm,\textup{st}}-F^{n}_{\pm,\textup{st}})(x,v)|\\
\lesssim\frac{1}{\beta m_{\pm}g}\|\mathrm{w}^{2}_{\pm,\beta}(\cdot,0,\cdot)\nabla_{x_{\parallel},v}G_{\pm}(\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x_{\parallel},v}}\left(\|(\mathbf{E}^{k-1}_{\textup{st}}-\mathbf{E}^{n-1}_{\textup{st}})(\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|(\mathbf{B}^{k-1}_{\textup{st}}-\mathbf{B}^{n-1}_{\textup{st}})(\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\right).

Since the representations (
4.4
) and (
4.5
) for
\mathbf{E}^{k-1}_{\textup{st}}
,
\mathbf{E}^{n-1}_{\textup{st}}
,
\mathbf{B}^{k-1}_{\textup{st}}
, and
\mathbf{B}^{n-1}_{\textup{st}}
are linear in
F_{\pm,\textup{st}}
, the differences
\mathbf{E}^{k-1}_{\textup{st}}-\mathbf{E}^{n-1}_{\textup{st}}
and
\mathbf{B}^{k-1}_{\textup{st}}-\mathbf{B}^{n-1}_{\textup{st}}
can be expressed in the same form, with
F_{\pm,\textup{st}}
replaced by
F^{k}_{\pm,\textup{st}}-F^{n}_{\pm,\textup{st}}
.
Therefore, we have

|\mathbf{E}^{k-1}_{\textup{st}}(x)-\mathbf{E}^{n-1}_{\textup{st}}(x)|\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\left|\nabla\mathfrak{G}_{\textup{odd}}(x,y)\right|\left|\sum_{\iota=\pm}\iota\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(F^{k-1}_{\iota,\textup{st}}-F^{n-1}_{\iota,\textup{st}})(y,v)dv\right|\ dy.

Then we further observe that

(4.39)

|\mathbf{E}^{k-1}_{\textup{st}}(x)-\mathbf{E}^{n-1}_{\textup{st}}(x)|\\
\lesssim\sum_{\iota=\pm}\frac{1}{\beta^{3}}\left(\sup_{x,v}e^{\frac{1}{2}\beta|x_{\parallel}|}e^{\frac{1}{4}\beta\left(v^{0}_{\iota}+m_{\iota}gx_{3}\right)}|(F^{k-1}_{\iota,\textup{st}}-F^{n-1}_{\iota,\textup{st}})(x,v)|\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}dy\ \left|\nabla\mathfrak{G}_{\textup{odd}}(x,y)\right|e^{-\frac{\beta}{2}|y_{\parallel}|}e^{-\frac{1}{2}m_{\iota}g\beta y_{3}},

by (
4.15
). Then since
\left|\nabla\mathfrak{G}_{\textup{odd}}(x,y)\right|\leq\frac{1}{|x-y|^{2}}+\frac{1}{|\bar{x}-y|^{2}}
and the upper bound is even in
y_{3}
, note that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}dy\ \left|\nabla\mathfrak{G}_{\textup{odd}}(x,y)\right|e^{-\frac{\beta}{2}|y_{\parallel}|}e^{-\frac{1}{2}m_{\pm}g\beta y_{3}}\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dy\ \left(\frac{1}{|x-y|^{2}}+\frac{1}{|\bar{x}-y|^{2}}\right)e^{-\frac{\beta}{2}|y_{\parallel}|}e^{-\frac{1}{2}m_{\pm}g\beta|y_{3}|}\\
\lesssim\frac{1}{m_{\pm}g\beta^{3}}\frac{1}{\langle x\rangle^{2}},

by (
4.16
). Since
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\hat{v}_{\pm,i}||F_{\pm,\textup{st}}(y,v)|dv\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|F_{\pm,\textup{st}}(y,v)|dv,
we also expect the same upper bound for the difference
|\mathbf{B}^{k-1}_{\textup{st}}(x)-\mathbf{B}^{n-1}_{\textup{st}}(x)|
. Therefore, we conclude that

(4.40)

|\mathbf{E}^{k-1}_{\textup{st}}(x)-\mathbf{E}^{n-1}_{\textup{st}}(x)|,|\mathbf{B}^{k-1}_{\textup{st}}(x)-\mathbf{B}^{n-1}_{\textup{st}}(x)|\\
\lesssim\frac{1}{\min\{m_{+},m_{-}\}g\beta^{6}}\max_{\iota=\pm}\left(\sup_{x,v}e^{\frac{1}{2}\beta|x_{\parallel}|}e^{\frac{1}{4}\beta\left(v^{0}_{\iota}+m_{\iota}gx_{3}\right)}|(F^{k-1}_{\iota,\textup{st}}-F^{n-1}_{\iota,\textup{st}})(x,v)|\right),

and hence by (
4.38
),

(4.41)

e^{\frac{1}{2}\beta|x_{\parallel}|}e^{\frac{1}{4}\beta\left(v^{0}_{\pm}+m_{\pm}gx_{3}\right)}|(F^{k}_{\pm,\textup{st}}-F^{n}_{\pm,\textup{st}})(x,v)|\lesssim\frac{1}{\min\{m^{2}_{+},m^{2}_{-}\}g^{2}\beta^{7}}\|\mathrm{w}^{2}_{\pm,\beta}(\cdot,0,\cdot)\nabla_{x_{\parallel},v}G_{\pm}(\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x_{\parallel},v}}\\
\times\max_{\iota=\pm}\left(\sup_{x,v}e^{\frac{1}{2}\beta|x_{\parallel}|}e^{\frac{1}{4}\beta\left(v^{0}_{\iota}+m_{\iota}gx_{3}\right)}|(F^{k-1}_{\iota,\textup{st}}-F^{n-1}_{\iota,\textup{st}})(x,v)|\right).

Note that for a sufficiently large
\beta\gg 1,
we have

\kappa\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}\frac{1}{\min\{m^{2}_{+},m^{2}_{-}\}g^{2}\beta^{7}}\|\mathrm{w}^{2}_{\pm,\beta}(\cdot,0,\cdot)\nabla_{x_{\parallel},v}G_{\pm}(\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x_{\parallel},v}}\ll 1.

Then by repeating the argument, we have

(4.42)

e^{\frac{1}{2}\beta|x_{\parallel}|}e^{\frac{1}{4}\beta\left(v^{0}_{\pm}+m_{\pm}gx_{3}\right)}|(F^{k}_{\pm,\textup{st}}-F^{n}_{\pm,\textup{st}})(x,v)|\\
\lesssim\kappa^{n}\max_{\iota=\pm}\left(\sup_{x,v}e^{\frac{1}{2}\beta|x_{\parallel}|}e^{\frac{1}{4}\beta\left(v^{0}_{\iota}+m_{\iota}gx_{3}\right)}|(F^{k-n}_{\iota,\textup{st}}-F^{0}_{\iota,\textup{st}})(x,v)|\right)\lesssim C\kappa^{n},

by the uniform estimate (
4.11
) and that
F^{0}_{\pm,\textup{st}}=0
. Therefore, we conclude that
\{F^{k}_{\pm,\textup{st}}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is Cauchy, and hence
\{(\mathbf{E}^{k}_{\textup{st}},\mathbf{B}^{k}_{\textup{st}})\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
are also Cauchy by (
4.40
). We record this fact in the following lemma:

Lemma 4.13
.

Both sequences
\{F^{k}_{\pm,\textup{st}}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\{(\mathbf{E}^{k}_{\textup{st}},\mathbf{B}^{k}_{\textup{st}})\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
are Cauchy.

Once the Cauchy property is verified as above, the same argument as in (
8.13
) applies to pass to the limit in the nonlinear terms via the strong convergence of Cauchy sequences.
Also, these solutions
(F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{\infty},\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{\infty},\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{\infty})
satisfy the same (weighted-)
L^{\infty}
bounds (
4.11
) as well as the uniform derivative estimate (
4.33
). This completes the proof of the existence of steady-states with Jüttner-Maxwell upper bounds (Theorem
2.1
).

4.5.
Uniqueness and Non-Negativity

We now establish the uniqueness of solutions to the stationary Vlasov–Maxwell system (
2.4
).

Suppose that there are two stationary solutions
(F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{(1)},\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{(1)},\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{(1)})
and
(F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{(2)},\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{(2)},\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{(2)})
for the system (
2.4
) under (
2.5
) and (
2.6
). Then note that we have

(4.43)

\begin{split}&(F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{(1)}-F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{(2)})(x_{\parallel},0,v)|_{\gamma_{-}}=0,\end{split}

and the difference
F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{(1)}-F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{(2)}
solves the following Vlasov equation:

(4.44)

\hat{v}_{\pm}\cdot\nabla_{x}(F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{(1)}-F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{(2)})+\left(\pm\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{(1)}\pm(\hat{v}_{\pm})\times\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{(1)}-m_{\pm}g\hat{e}_{3}\right)\cdot\nabla_{v}(F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{(1)}-F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{(2)})\\
=-\left(\pm(\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{(1)}-\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{(2)})\pm(\hat{v}_{\pm})\times(\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{(1)}-\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{(2)})\right)\cdot\nabla_{v}F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{(2)}.

Similarly to (
3.39
), we define the stationary characteristic trajectory variables

Z_{\pm,\textup{st}}(s)=(X_{\pm,\textup{st}}(s),V_{\pm,\textup{st}}(s))
satisfying
Z_{\pm,\textup{st}}(0;x,v)=(X_{\pm,\textup{st}}(0;x,v),V_{\pm,\textup{st}}(0;x,v))=(x,v)=z
, generated by the fields
\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{(1)}
and
\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{(1)}
, which solves

\begin{split}\frac{dX_{\pm,\textup{st}}(s)}{ds}&=\hat{V}_{\pm,\textup{st}}(s)=\frac{V_{\pm,\textup{st}}(s)}{\sqrt{m_{\pm}^{2}+|V_{\pm,\textup{st}}(s)|^{2}}},\\
\frac{dV_{\pm,\textup{st}}(s)}{ds}&=\pm\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{(1)}(X_{\pm,\textup{st}}(s))\pm\hat{V}_{\pm,\textup{st}}(s)\times\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{(1)}(X_{\pm,\textup{st}}(s))-m_{\pm}g\hat{e}_{3},\end{split}

where
\hat{e}_{3}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}(0,0,1)^{\top}
and
\hat{v}_{\pm}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}\frac{v}{v^{0}_{\pm}}=\frac{v}{\sqrt{m_{\pm}^{2}+|v|^{2}}}
.
In addition, similarly to (
3.45
), denote the corresponding forward and backward and exit times
t_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{f}}
and
t_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}
for the steady characteristic trajectory as

\begin{split}&t_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{f}}(x,v)=\sup\{s\in[0,\infty):(X_{\pm,\textup{st}})_{3}(\tau;x,v)>0\ \textup{ for all }\tau\in(0,s)\}\geq 0,\\
&t_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}(x,v)=\sup\{s\in[0,\infty):(X_{\pm,\textup{st}})_{3}(-\tau;x,v)>0\ \textup{ for all }\tau\in(0,s)\}\geq 0.\end{split}

Then, by integrating (
4.44
) along the characteristics
Z_{\pm,\textup{st}}(s)=(X_{\pm,\textup{st}}(s),V_{\pm,\textup{st}}(s))
(associated with
\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{(1)}
and
\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{(1)}
) for
s\in[-t_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}},0]
, we obtain

(4.45)

(F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{(1)}-F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{(2)})(x,v)=\mp\int^{0}_{-t_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}}\left((\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{(1)}-\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{(2)})(X_{\pm,\textup{st}}(s))+\hat{V}_{\pm,\textup{st}}(s)\times(\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{(1)}-\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{(2)})(X_{\pm,\textup{st}}(s))\right)\\
\cdot\nabla_{v}F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{(2)}(X_{\pm,\textup{st}}(s),V_{\pm,\textup{st}}(s))ds.

Therefore, we obtain

(4.46)

|(F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{(1)}-F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{(2)})(x,v)|\\
\leq t_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}\sup_{s\in[-t_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}},0]}|(\nabla_{v}F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{(2)})(X_{\pm,\textup{st}}(s),V_{\pm,\textup{st}}(s))|\left(\|(\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{(1)}-\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{(2)})(\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|(\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{(1)}-\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{(2)})(\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\right).

Regarding the momentum derivative
\nabla_{v}F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{(2)}
,
we use the uniform estimate (
4.33
) and obtain that

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[-t_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}},0]}|(\nabla_{v}F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{(2)})(X_{\pm,\textup{st}}(s),V_{\pm,\textup{st}}(s))|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{s\in[-t_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}},0]}\frac{1}{\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}(X_{\pm,\textup{st}}(s),V_{\pm,\textup{st}}(s))}|(\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}\nabla_{v}F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{(2)})(X_{\pm,\textup{st}}(s),V_{\pm,\textup{st}}(s))|

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{-\frac{1}{2}\beta\left(v^{0}_{\pm}+m_{\pm}gx_{3}+|x_{\parallel}|\right)}\|\mathrm{w}^{2}_{\pm,\beta}(\cdot,0,\cdot)\nabla_{x_{\parallel},v}G_{\pm}(\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x_{\parallel},v}},

by the weight comparison estimate (
3.53
) along the steady characteristic trajectory
(X_{\pm,\textup{st}}(s),V_{\pm,\textup{st}}(s)).

In addition, note that by (
3.51
) we have

t_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}\leq\frac{32}{13m_{\pm}g}(\sqrt{m_{\pm}^{2}+|v_{\pm}|^{2}}+m_{\pm}gx_{3})\leq\frac{3}{m_{\pm}g}\left(v^{0}_{\pm}+m_{\pm}gx_{3}\right).

Regarding the upper bound of
t_{\pm,\textup{st},\mathbf{b}}
, we further observe that

(v^{0}_{\pm}+m_{\pm}gx_{3})e^{-\frac{1}{2}\beta\left(v^{0}_{\pm}+m_{\pm}gx_{3}+|x_{\parallel}|\right)}\lesssim\frac{1}{\beta}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\beta|x_{\parallel}|}e^{-\frac{1}{4}\beta\left(v^{0}_{\pm}+m_{\pm}gx_{3}\right)}.

Therefore, by (
4.46
), we have

(4.47)

e^{\frac{1}{2}\beta|x_{\parallel}|}e^{\frac{1}{4}\beta\left(v^{0}_{\pm}+m_{\pm}gx_{3}\right)}|(F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{(1)}-F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{(2)})(x,v)|\\
\lesssim\frac{1}{\beta m_{\pm}g}\|\mathrm{w}^{2}_{\pm,\beta}(\cdot,0,\cdot)\nabla_{x_{\parallel},v}G_{\pm}(\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x_{\parallel},v}}\left(\|(\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{(1)}-\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{(2)})(\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|(\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{(1)}-\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{(2)})(\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\right).

We now derive upper bounds for the differences
\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{(1)}-\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{(2)}
and
\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{(1)}-\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{(2)}
. Our objective is to obtain uniform estimates for these quantities in terms of

(4.48)

e^{\frac{1}{2}\beta|x_{\parallel}|}e^{\frac{1}{4}\beta\left(v^{0}_{\pm}+m_{\pm}gx_{3}\right)}|(F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{(1)}-F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{(2)})(x,v)|.

Recall that we use the representations given in (
4.4
) and (
4.5
) for
\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{(1)}
,
\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{(2)}
,
\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{(1)}
, and
\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{(2)}
. Since these representations are linear in
F_{\pm,\textup{st}}
, the differences
\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{(1)}-\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{(2)}
and
\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{(1)}-\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{(2)}
can be expressed in the same form, with
F_{\pm,\textup{st}}
replaced by
F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{(1)}-F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{(2)}
.
Therefore, we have

|\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{(1)}(x)-\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{(2)}(x)|\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}|\nabla\mathfrak{G}_{\textup{odd}}(x,y)|\left|\sum_{\iota=\pm}\iota\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(F_{\iota,\textup{st}}^{(1)}-F_{\iota,\textup{st}}^{(2)})(y,v)dv\right|\ dy.

By factoring out the term (
4.48
), we further observe that

(4.49)

|\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{(1)}(x)-\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{(2)}(x)|\\
\lesssim\sum_{\iota=\pm}\frac{1}{\beta^{3}}\left(\sup_{x,v}e^{\frac{1}{2}\beta|x_{\parallel}|}e^{\frac{1}{4}\beta\left(v^{0}_{\iota}+m_{\iota}gx_{3}\right)}|(F_{\iota,\textup{st}}^{(1)}-F_{\iota,\textup{st}}^{(2)})(x,v)|\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}dy\ |\nabla\mathfrak{G}_{\textup{odd}}(x,y)|e^{-\frac{\beta}{2}|y_{\parallel}|}e^{-\frac{1}{2}m_{\iota}g\beta y_{3}},

by (
4.15
). Then since
|\nabla\mathfrak{G}_{\textup{odd}}(x,y)|\leq\frac{1}{|x-y|^{2}}+\frac{1}{|\bar{x}-y|^{2}}
and the upper bound is even in
y_{3}
, note that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}dy\ |\nabla\mathfrak{G}_{\textup{odd}}(x,y)|e^{-\frac{\beta}{2}|y_{\parallel}|}e^{-\frac{1}{2}m_{\pm}g\beta y_{3}}\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dy\ \left(\frac{1}{|x-y|^{2}}+\frac{1}{|\bar{x}-y|^{2}}\right)e^{-\frac{\beta}{2}|y_{\parallel}|}e^{-\frac{1}{2}m_{\pm}g\beta|y_{3}|}\\
\lesssim\frac{1}{m_{\pm}g\beta^{3}}\frac{1}{\langle x\rangle^{2}},

by (
4.16
). Therefore, we conclude that

(4.50)

|\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{(1)}(x)-\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{(2)}(x)|\lesssim\frac{1}{\min\{m_{+},m_{-}\}g\beta^{6}}\max_{\iota=\pm}\left(\sup_{x,v}e^{\frac{1}{2}\beta|x_{\parallel}|}e^{\frac{1}{4}\beta\left(v^{0}_{\iota}+m_{\iota}gx_{3}\right)}|(F_{\iota,\textup{st}}^{(1)}-F_{\iota,\textup{st}}^{(2)})(x,v)|\right).

Moreover, since
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\hat{v}_{\pm,i}||F_{\pm,\textup{st}}(y,v)|dv\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|F_{\pm,\textup{st}}(y,v)|dv,

\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}(x)
in (
4.4
) also has the same upper-bound (up to constant) as that of
\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}(x)
and hence we have the same upper bound on the difference

|\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{(1)}(x)-\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{(2)}(x)|\lesssim\frac{1}{\min\{m_{+},m_{-}\}g\beta^{6}}\max_{\iota=\pm}\left(\sup_{x,v}e^{\frac{1}{2}\beta|x_{\parallel}|}e^{\frac{1}{4}\beta\left(v^{0}_{\iota}+m_{\iota}gx_{3}\right)}|(F_{\iota,\textup{st}}^{(1)}-F_{\iota,\textup{st}}^{(2)})(x,v)|\right).

Consequently, by (
4.47
), we obtain that

(4.51)

\sup_{x,v}e^{\frac{1}{2}\beta|x_{\parallel}|}e^{\frac{1}{4}\beta\left(v^{0}_{\pm}+m_{\pm}gx_{3}\right)}|(F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{(1)}-F_{\pm,\textup{st}}^{(2)})(x,v)|\\
\lesssim\frac{1}{\min\{m^{2}_{+},m^{2}_{-}\}g^{2}\beta^{7}}\|\mathrm{w}^{2}_{\pm,\beta}(\cdot,0,\cdot)\nabla_{x_{\parallel},v}G_{\pm}(\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x_{\parallel},v}}\max_{\iota=\pm}\left(\sup_{x,v}e^{\frac{1}{2}\beta|x_{\parallel}|}e^{\frac{1}{4}\beta\left(v^{0}_{\iota}+m_{\iota}gx_{3}\right)}|(F_{\iota,\textup{st}}^{(1)}-F_{\iota,\textup{st}}^{(2)})(x,v)|\right).

By choosing
\beta\gg 1
sufficiently large, we conclude that

\max_{\iota=\pm}\left(\sup_{x,v}e^{\frac{1}{2}\beta|x_{\parallel}|}e^{\frac{1}{4}\beta\left(v^{0}_{\iota}+m_{\iota}gx_{3}\right)}|(F_{\iota,\textup{st}}^{(1)}-F_{\iota,\textup{st}}^{(2)})(x,v)|\right)=0,

and hence

|\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{(1)}(x)-\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}^{(2)}(x)|=|\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{(1)}(x)-\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}^{(2)}(x)|=0,\text{ for any }x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}.

This completes the proof of uniqueness for the stationary solution.

Next we address the non-negativity of the solution we have contructed. Assume that the inflow boundary profile
G_{\pm}
is non-negative. Since
F_{\pm,\textup{st}}
remains constant along the stationary characteristics described by (
3.39
), it follows that
F_{\pm,\textup{st}}
is also non-negative.

This concludes our analysis of the existence and uniform estimates for steady states under Jüttner-Maxwell upper bounds. In the next section, we explore perturbative solutions around these steady states and establish their asymptotic stability using a bootstrap argument.

5.
Dynamical Asymptotic Stability

In this section, we establish the asymptotic stability of the steady states
(F_{\pm,\textup{st}},\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}},\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}})
, whose unique existence is guaranteed by Theorem
2.1
.
We show that the perturbation
(f_{\pm},\mathscr{E},\mathscr{B})
from the steady state decays linearly in time, thereby concluding that the stationary states are asymptotically stable.

We assume that the inflow boundary data
G_{\pm}
at
x_{3}=0
coincide with the stationary states
F_{\pm,\textup{st}}
for incoming particles with
v\in{\mathbb{R}^{3}}
such that
n_{x}\cdot v<0
.
Recall that these profiles are bounded above by Jüttner equilibrium distributions (
2.11
).
As before, we suppose that
\mathbf{E}
,
\mathbf{B}
,
\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}
, and
\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}
(and thus also
\mathscr{E}
and
\mathscr{B}
) satisfy the perfect conductor boundary condition (
1.4
) on
x_{3}=0
.

5.1.
Perturbations from the Steady States

We first define the perturbation
(f_{\pm},\mathscr{E},\mathscr{B})
from the steady-state
(F_{\pm,\textup{st}},\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}},\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}})
:

Definition 5.1
.

Define the perturbations
(f_{\pm},\mathscr{E},\mathscr{B})
from the steady-state
(F_{\pm,\textup{st}},\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}},\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}})
as

(5.1)

\begin{split}f_{\pm}(t,x,v)&\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}F_{\pm}(t,x,v)-F_{\pm,\textup{st}}(x,v),\ \mathscr{E}(t,x)\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}\mathbf{E}(t,x)-\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}(x),\textup{ and }\mathscr{B}(t,x)\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}\mathbf{B}(t,x)-\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}(x),\end{split}

for
t\in[0,\infty)
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+},
and
v\in{\mathbb{R}^{3}}
where the full solution
(F_{\pm},\mathbf{E},\mathbf{B})
and the steady-state
(F_{\pm,\textup{st}},\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}},\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}})
solve the dynamical and the stationary systems of the Vlasov–Maxwell equations (
1.1
) and (
2.4
), respectively, in the sense of distributions.

Then by (
1.1
) and (
2.4
), we observe that the perturbations
(f_{\pm},\mathscr{E},\mathscr{B})
solve the perturbative system of Vlasov–Maxwell equations (
2.1
) (with
c=1
and
\textup{e}=1
normalized)
where
\varrho
and
\mathscr{J}
are defined as (
2.3
) and satisfy the continuity equation

(5.2)

\partial_{t}\varrho+\nabla_{x}\cdot\mathscr{J}=0.

Under the assumptions above, we consider an iterated sequence of solutions
(f^{l}_{\pm},\mathscr{E}^{l},\mathscr{B}^{l})
that solves the following Vlasov–Maxwell system under (
1.5
). Note that we can consider the same characteristic trajectory
\mathscr{Z}^{l}_{\pm}=(\mathscr{X}^{l}_{\pm},\mathscr{V}^{l}_{\pm})
solving (
5.5
) but now in the whole half space
\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}
. The iterated sequence of solutions
(f^{l}_{\pm},\mathscr{E}^{l},\mathscr{B}^{l})
solve

(5.3)

\begin{split}\partial_{t}f^{l+1}_{\pm}+\hat{v}_{\pm}\cdot\nabla_{x}f^{l+1}_{\pm}+\left(\pm\mathbf{E}^{l}\pm\hat{v}_{\pm}\times\mathbf{B}^{l}-m_{\pm}g\hat{e}_{3}\right)\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{l+1}_{\pm}&=\mp\left(\mathscr{E}^{l}+\hat{v}_{\pm}\times\mathscr{B}^{l}\right)\cdot\nabla_{v}F_{\pm,\textup{st}},\\
f^{l+1}_{\pm}(0,x,v)=f^{\textup{in}}_{\pm}(x,v),\ f^{l+1}_{\pm}(t,x_{\parallel},0,v)|_{\gamma_{-}}&=0,\text{ and }\end{split}

(5.4)

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\mathscr{E}^{l}-\nabla_{x}\times\mathscr{B}^{l}&=-4\pi\mathscr{J}^{l},\ \partial_{t}\mathscr{B}^{l}+\nabla_{x}\times\mathscr{E}^{l}=0,\\
\nabla_{x}\cdot\mathscr{E}^{l}&=4\pi\varrho^{l},\ \nabla_{x}\cdot\mathscr{B}^{l}=0.\end{split}

By (
3.38
), we can also define the characteristic trajectory
\mathscr{Z}^{l+1}_{\pm}=(\mathscr{X}^{l+1}_{\pm},\mathscr{V}^{l+1}_{\pm})
which solves

(5.5)

\begin{split}\frac{d\mathscr{X}^{l+1}_{\pm}(s)}{ds}&=\hat{\mathscr{V}}^{l+1}_{\pm}(s)=\frac{\mathscr{V}^{l+1}_{\pm}(s)}{\sqrt{m_{\pm}^{2}+|\mathscr{V}^{l+1}_{\pm}(s)|^{2}}},\\
\frac{d\mathscr{V}^{l+1}_{\pm}(s)}{ds}&=\pm\mathbf{E}^{l}(s,\mathscr{X}^{l+1}_{\pm}(s))\pm\hat{\mathscr{V}}^{l+1}_{\pm}(s)\times\mathbf{B}^{l}(s,\mathscr{X}^{l+1}_{\pm}(s))-m_{\pm}g\hat{e}_{3},\end{split}

where
\mathscr{X}^{l+1}_{\pm}(s)=\mathscr{X}^{l+1}_{\pm}(s;t,x,v)
,
\mathscr{V}^{l+1}_{\pm}(s)=\mathscr{V}^{l+1}_{\pm}(s;t,x,v)
,
\hat{e}_{3}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}(0,0,1)^{\top}
, and
(\hat{v}_{\pm})\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}\frac{v}{\sqrt{m_{\pm}^{2}+|v|^{2}}}
.

Our main goal is to prove that the perturbations
(f_{\pm},\mathscr{E},\mathscr{B})
decay linearly in time. In particular, the argument controls nonlinear terms while simultaneously extracting decay from the linearized dynamics, thereby establishing full nonlinear asymptotic stability.
In the subsequent sections, we establish decay-in-time estimates for these iterates and close the nonlinear argument to obtain asymptotic stability.

In the rest of the section, we prove the following main proposition on the linear-in-time decay of the perturbations:

Proposition 5.2
.

For any
l\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

(5.6)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\ \langle t\rangle

\displaystyle\left\|e^{\frac{\beta}{2}|x_{\parallel}|}e^{\frac{\beta}{4}(v^{0}_{\pm}+m_{\pm}gx_{3})}f^{l}_{\pm}(t,\cdot,\cdot)\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4}{\beta}\bigg(\|\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}f^{\textup{in}}_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+C\min\{m_{-},m_{+}\}\frac{g}{8}\|\mathrm{w}^{2}_{\pm,\beta}(\cdot,0,\cdot)\nabla_{x_{\parallel},v}G_{\pm}(\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x_{\parallel},v}}\bigg),

(5.7)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\ \langle t\rangle\|(\mathscr{E}^{l},\mathscr{B}^{l})\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\min\{m_{-},m_{+}\}\frac{g}{16},

for a sufficiently large
\beta>1
where the weight
\mathrm{w}=\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}
is defined as (
3.46
).

In the following sections, we fix
l\in\mathbb{N}
and assume that (
5.6
)–(
5.7
) hold at the iteration level
(l)
. We then show that these same estimates remain valid at the next level
(l+1)
, thereby closing the bootstrap argument.

Proof of Proposition
5.2
.

Proposition
5.2
follows from Lemma
5.4
and Lemma
5.6
, which will be established in the subsequent sections.
∎

Remark 5.3

(Compatibility Conditions)
.

For the limiting weak solution we impose only the perfect–conductor Dirichlet data on

\mathbf{E}_{1},\ \mathbf{E}_{2},\ \mathbf{B}_{3},

while the Neumann-type relations for

\mathbf{E}_{3},\ \mathbf{B}_{1},\ \mathbf{B}_{2}

are used only at the approximate level and are encoded in the weak formulation; no additional boundary conditions are imposed on the limit, and with

W^{1,\infty}

regularity this suffices to define the trace at

x_{3}=0

and to close all boundary terms consistently with the continuity equation and the wave system.

The Neumann boundary conditions for the iterated sequence
\mathbf{E}^{l+1}_{3}
,
\mathbf{B}^{l+1}_{1}
, and
\mathbf{B}^{l+1}_{2}
can be formally derived and be justified. We first impose the Dirichlet-type perfect conductor boundary conditions (
1.4
) to the iterated fields
\mathbf{E}^{l+1}_{1},

\mathbf{E}^{l+1}_{2}
and
\mathbf{B}^{l+1}_{3}
.
Then using the Gauss’s law, we obtain that

\partial_{x_{3}}\mathbf{E}^{l+1}_{3}=4\pi\rho^{l+1}-\partial_{x_{1}}\mathbf{E}^{l+1}_{1}-\partial_{x_{2}}\mathbf{E}^{l+1}_{2}.

Formally (needs some justification that
\partial_{x_{1}}\mathbf{E}^{l+1}_{1},\partial_{x_{2}}\mathbf{E}^{l+1}_{2},4\pi\rho^{l+1}
have their traces in a proper space such as
C^{0}(\bar{\Omega})
at the sequential level of construction of solutions), we have
\partial_{x_{1}}\mathbf{E}^{l+1}_{1}=0=\partial_{x_{2}}\mathbf{E}^{l+1}_{2}
from (
1.4
). Hence
\mathbf{E}^{l+1}_{3}
formally satisfies the Neumann boundary condition:

(\partial_{x_{3}}\mathbf{E}^{l+1}_{3}-4\pi\rho^{l+1})|_{\partial\Omega}=0.

Also, using the Ampère-Maxwell equation, we derive that

\displaystyle n\times(\nabla\times\mathbf{B}^{l+1})-4\pi n\times J^{l+1}=n\times\partial_{t}\mathbf{E}^{l+1}

for any
n\in{\mathbb{R}^{3}}.

In addition, from (
1.4
), we formally (needs some justification that
\partial_{t}\mathbf{E}^{l+1}_{\parallel},\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}\mathbf{B}^{l+1}_{3},
and
J_{\parallel}
have their traces in a proper space such as
C^{0}(\bar{\Omega})
at the sequential level of construction of solutions) have
\partial_{t}\mathbf{E}^{l+1}_{1}=0=\partial_{t}\mathbf{E}^{l+1}_{2}
at
\partial\Omega
, and
\partial_{x_{1}}\mathbf{B}^{l+1}_{3}=0=\partial_{x_{2}}\mathbf{B}^{l+1}_{3}
at
\partial\Omega
. Then by choosing
n
to be the outward normal vector at the boundary
x_{3}=0
as
n=(0,0,-1)^{\top},
formally we derive that

\begin{bmatrix}0\\
0\\
-1\end{bmatrix}\times\begin{bmatrix}\partial_{x_{2}}\mathbf{B}^{l+1}_{3}-\partial_{x_{3}}\mathbf{B}^{l+1}_{2}\\
-(\partial_{x_{1}}\mathbf{B}^{l+1}_{3}-\partial_{x_{3}}\mathbf{B}^{l+1}_{1})\\
\partial_{x_{1}}\mathbf{B}^{l+1}_{2}-\partial_{x_{2}}\mathbf{B}^{l+1}_{1}\end{bmatrix}-4\pi\begin{bmatrix}J^{l+1}_{2}\\
-J^{l+1}_{1}\\
0\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}\partial_{t}\mathbf{E}^{l+1}_{2}\\
-\partial_{t}\mathbf{E}^{l+1}_{1}\\
0\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}0\\
0\\
0\end{bmatrix}\ \ \textup{at $\partial\Omega$, }

and hence

\displaystyle(\partial_{x_{3}}\mathbf{B}^{l+1}_{1}-4\pi J^{l+1}_{2})|_{\partial\Omega}=0,\ \ (\partial_{x_{3}}\mathbf{B}^{l+1}_{2}+4\pi J^{l+1}_{1})|_{\partial\Omega}=0.

Therefore, we have

(5.8)

\partial_{x_{3}}\mathbf{E}_{3}=4\pi\rho,\quad\partial_{x_{3}}\mathbf{B}_{2}=-4\pi J_{1},\quad\textup{and}\quad\partial_{x_{3}}\mathbf{B}_{1}=4\pi J_{2}.

Note that the Neumann conditions above are not really boundary conditions. They are the identities that the smooth solution should satisfy at the boundary as long as all the quantities have a proper sense of trace at the boundary.

5.2.
Enhanced Decay-in-
t
for the Distributions and Fields

In this section, we prove the estimate (
5.6
) at the iteration level
(l+1)
.

Lemma 5.4
.

Fix
l\in\mathbb{N}
and suppose (
5.7
) hold for
(\mathscr{E}^{l},\mathscr{B}^{l}).
Then
f^{l+1}_{\pm}
satisfies

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\ \langle t\rangle

\displaystyle\left\|e^{\frac{\beta}{2}|x_{\parallel}|}e^{\frac{\beta}{4}(v^{0}_{\pm}+m_{\pm}gx_{3})}f^{l+1}_{\pm}(t,\cdot,\cdot)\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4}{\beta}\bigg(\|\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}f^{\textup{in}}_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+C\min\{m_{-},m_{+}\}\frac{g}{8}\|\mathrm{w}^{2}_{\pm,\beta}(\cdot,0,\cdot)\nabla_{x_{\parallel},v}G_{\pm}(\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x_{\parallel},v}}\bigg).

Proof.

By writing the solution
f^{l+1}_{\pm}
in the mild form

(5.9)

f^{l+1}_{\pm}(t,x,v)=1_{t\leq t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}(t,x,v)}f^{\textup{in}}_{\pm}(\mathscr{X}^{l+1}_{\pm}(0;t,x,v),\mathscr{V}^{l+1}_{\pm}(0;t,x,v))\\
\mp\int^{t}_{\max\{0,t-t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}\}}\left(\mathscr{E}^{l}(s,\mathscr{X}^{l+1}_{\pm}(s))+\hat{\mathscr{V}}^{l+1}_{\pm}(s)\times\mathscr{B}^{l}(s,\mathscr{X}^{l+1}_{\pm}(s))\right)\cdot\nabla_{v}F_{\pm,\textup{st}}(\mathscr{X}^{l+1}_{\pm}(s),\mathscr{V}^{l+1}_{\pm}(s))ds,

and using (
5.7
), we obtain

\displaystyle\langle t\rangle|f^{l+1}_{\pm}(t,x,v)|

\displaystyle\leq\langle t\rangle\frac{1_{t\leq t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}(t,x,v)}}{\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}(\mathscr{Z}^{l+1}_{\pm}(0;t,x,v))}\|\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}f^{\textup{in}}_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle+1_{t\leq t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}(t,x,v)}\langle t\rangle\|(\mathscr{E}^{l},\mathscr{B}^{l})\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}\nabla_{v}F_{\pm,\textup{st}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\int^{t}_{0}\frac{1}{\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}(\mathscr{Z}^{l+1}_{\pm}(s;t,x,v))}ds

\displaystyle+1_{t>t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}(t,x,v)}\langle t\rangle\|(\mathscr{E}^{l},\mathscr{B}^{l})\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}\nabla_{v}F_{\pm,\textup{st}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\int^{t}_{t-t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}}\frac{1}{\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}(\mathscr{Z}^{l+1}_{\pm}(s;t,x,v))}ds

\displaystyle\leq\langle t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}\rangle e^{-\frac{1}{2}\beta v_{\pm}^{0}-\frac{1}{2}m_{\pm}g\beta x_{3}-\frac{\beta}{2}|x_{\parallel}|}\|\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}f^{\textup{in}}_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle+\langle t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}\rangle 1_{t\leq t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}(t,x,v)}\min\{m_{-},m_{+}\}\frac{g}{8}\|\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}\nabla_{v}F_{\pm,\textup{st}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\beta v_{\pm}^{0}-\frac{1}{2}m_{\pm}g\beta x_{3}-\frac{\beta}{2}|x_{\parallel}|}

\displaystyle+t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}1_{t>t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}(t,x,v)}\min\{m_{-},m_{+}\}\frac{g}{8}\|\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}\nabla_{v}F_{\pm,\textup{st}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\beta v_{\pm}^{0}-\frac{1}{2}m_{\pm}g\beta x_{3}-\frac{\beta}{2}|x_{\parallel}|},

by(
3.56
) and (
3.58
). Then using (
3.56
) and (
4.33
) with
G_{\pm}
replaced by
\delta G_{\pm}
, we further have

(5.10)

\begin{split}&\langle t\rangle|f^{l+1}_{\pm}(t,x,v)|\\
&\leq\left(1+\frac{16}{5m_{\pm}g}(v_{\pm}^{0}+m_{\pm}gx_{3})\right)e^{-\frac{1}{2}\beta v_{\pm}^{0}-\frac{1}{2}m_{\pm}g\beta x_{3}-\frac{\beta}{2}|x_{\parallel}|}\\
&\qquad\times\bigg(\|\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}f^{\textup{in}}_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+\min\{m_{-},m_{+}\}\frac{g}{8}\|\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}\nabla_{v}F_{\pm,\textup{st}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\bigg)\\
&\leq\frac{11}{10\beta}(\beta v_{\pm}^{0}+m_{\pm}g\beta x_{3})e^{-\frac{1}{2}\beta v_{\pm}^{0}-\frac{1}{2}m_{\pm}g\beta x_{3}-\frac{\beta}{2}|x_{\parallel}|}\bigg(\|\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}f^{\textup{in}}_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+\min\{m_{-},m_{+}\}\frac{g}{8}\|\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}\nabla_{v}F_{\pm,\textup{st}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\bigg)\\
&\leq\frac{44}{5\beta e}e^{-\frac{1}{4}\beta v_{\pm}^{0}-\frac{1}{4}m_{\pm}g\beta x_{3}-\frac{\beta}{2}|x_{\parallel}|}\bigg(\|\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}f^{\textup{in}}_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+C\min\{m_{-},m_{+}\}\frac{g}{8}\|\mathrm{w}^{2}_{\pm,\beta}(\cdot,0,\cdot)\nabla_{x_{\parallel},v}G_{\pm}(\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x_{\parallel},v}}\bigg),\end{split}

for
g>0
such that

\min\{m_{-},m_{+}\}g\geq 32.
Here, we also used the inequality that for
z\geq 0,

\beta ze^{-\frac{\beta}{2}z}\leq\frac{4}{e}e^{-\frac{\beta}{4}z}.

This completes the proof of Lemma
5.4
for
f^{l+1}_{\pm}.
∎

Now we prove the estimate (
5.7
) at the iteration level
(l+1)
. This estimate ensures an additional linear decay in time for the perturbations
\mathscr{E}^{l+1}
and
\mathscr{B}^{l+1}
, thereby implying the asymptotic stability of the steady states
\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}
and
\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}
. Recall that the total fields are given by

\mathbf{E}^{l+1}=\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}+\mathscr{E}^{l+1}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbf{B}^{l+1}=\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}+\mathscr{B}^{l+1},

where
\mathscr{E}^{l+1}
and
\mathscr{B}^{l+1}
represent perturbations around the steady states
\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}}
and
\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}}
, respectively.

Field representations for the perturbative fields
\mathscr{E}^{l+1}
and
\mathscr{B}^{l+1}

For the estimates of the perturbative components
\mathscr{E}^{l+1}
and
\mathscr{B}^{l+1}
, we employ the field representations of the electromagnetic fields given in (
A.1
), (
A.4
), (
3.33
), and (
3.37
), which were derived from the corresponding wave equations. We note that the Maxwell system (
5.4
) governing the perturbations
\mathscr{E}^{l+1}
and
\mathscr{B}^{l+1}
has the same structure as the full Maxwell system (
1.1
) for
\mathbf{E}
and
\mathbf{B}
, provided that
F
is replaced by
f^{l+1}_{\pm}
(and consequently
\rho
and
J
are replaced by
\varrho
and
\mathscr{J}
, respectively). Under this replacement, the only difference that affects the final representation lies in the nonlinear
S
-term. Specifically, when constructing the field representations for
\mathscr{E}^{l+1}
and
\mathscr{B}^{l+1}
, we use the inhomogeneous Vlasov equation (
5.3
) for
f^{l+1}_{\pm}
, which contains an additional inhomogeneity

-\nabla_{v}\cdot\Big((\pm\mathscr{E}^{l}\pm\hat{v}_{\pm}\times\mathscr{B}^{l})F_{\pm,\textup{st}}\Big).

This term introduces a new nonlinearity that appears only in the electric field representation, since our new derivation of the magnetic field representation shows that no nonlinear
S
-term arises via cancellation. Therefore, the perturbative fields
\mathscr{E}^{l+1}
and
\mathscr{B}^{l+1}
can be expressed as follows:
for each
i=1,2,3
,

\mathscr{E}^{l+1}_{i}=\mathscr{E}^{l+1}_{\textup{hom},i}+\mathscr{E}^{l+1}_{ib1}+\mathscr{E}^{l+1}_{ib2}+\mathscr{E}^{l+1}_{iT}+\mathscr{E}^{l+1}_{iS}+\delta_{i3}\mathscr{E}^{l+1}_{\textup{add},3},\quad\mathscr{B}^{l+1}_{i}=\mathscr{B}^{l+1}_{\textup{hom},i}+\mathscr{B}^{l+1}_{ib1}+\mathscr{B}^{l+1}_{iT},

where the terms
\mathscr{E}^{l+1}_{\textup{hom},i},\ \mathscr{E}^{l+1}_{ib1},\ \mathscr{E}^{l+1}_{ib2},\ \mathscr{E}^{l+1}_{iT},\ \mathscr{B}^{l+1}_{\textup{hom},i},\ \mathscr{B}^{l+1}_{ib1},
and
\mathscr{B}^{l+1}_{iT}
are given by the same representations as in (
A.1
), (
A.4
), (
3.33
), and (
3.37
), respectively, with
F_{\pm}
replaced by
f^{l+1}_{\pm}
. Note that the normal electric field contains an additional term
\mathscr{E}^{l+1}_{\textup{add},3}
defined as

\mathscr{E}^{l+1}_{\textup{add},3}(t,x)=\sum_{\iota=\pm}(-\iota)2\int_{B(x;t)\cap\{y_{3}=0\}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{f^{l+1}_{\iota}(t-|y-x|,y_{\parallel},0,v)}{|y-x|}\,dv\,dy_{\parallel}.

As noted above, the nonlinear
S
-term
\mathscr{E}^{l+1}_{iS}
for
i=1,2,3
arises not only from the nonlinear source

-\nabla_{v}\cdot\Big((\pm\mathbf{E}^{l}\pm\hat{v}_{\pm}\times\mathbf{B}^{l}-m_{\pm}g\hat{e}_{3})\,f^{l+1}_{\pm}\Big),

but also from the additional
inhomogeneous
stationary source

-\nabla_{v}\cdot\Big((\pm\mathscr{E}^{l}\pm\hat{v}_{\pm}\times\mathscr{B}^{l})\,F_{\pm,\textup{st}}\Big),

which appears in the equation for
f^{l+1}_{\pm}
in (
5.3
).
Therefore, for each
i=1,2,3,
, we further write
\mathscr{E}^{l+1}_{iS}
as a sum of two parts:

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{l+1}_{iS}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\pm}((\mathscr{E}^{l+1})^{(1)}_{\pm,iS}-(\mathscr{E}^{l+1})^{(2)}_{\pm,iS}),\ \text{ for $i=1,2$, and }

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{l+1}_{iS}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\pm}((\mathscr{E}^{l+1})^{(1)}_{\pm,iS}+(\mathscr{E}^{l+1})^{(2)}_{\pm,iS}),\ \text{ for $i=3$, }

where, with
a^{\mathbf{E}}_{\pm,i}
defined as (
A.2
),

\begin{split}(\mathscr{E}^{l+1})^{(1)}_{\pm,iS}(t,x)&=\pm\int_{B^{+}(x;t)}dy\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dv\,a^{\mathbf{E}}_{\pm,i}(v,\omega)\cdot(\pm\mathbf{E}^{l}\pm\hat{v}_{\pm}\times\mathbf{B}^{l}-m_{\pm}g\hat{e}_{3})\frac{f^{l+1}_{\pm}(t-|x-y|,y,v)}{|x-y|}\\
&\quad\mp\int_{B^{+}(x;t)}\frac{dy}{|y-x|}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dv\ \frac{\omega+\hat{v}_{\pm}}{1+\hat{v}_{\pm}\cdot\omega}\,\left(\pm\mathscr{E}\pm\hat{v}_{\pm}\times\mathscr{B}\right)\left(t-|x-y|,y\right)\cdot\nabla_{v}F_{\pm,\textup{st}}(y,v)\\
&\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}(\mathscr{E}^{l+1})^{(1),\textup{acc}}_{\pm,iS}(t,x)+(\mathscr{E}^{l+1})^{(1),\textup{st}}_{\pm,iS}(t,x),\text{ and }\\
(\mathscr{E}^{l+1})^{(2)}_{\pm,iS}(t,x)&=\pm\int_{B^{-}(x;t)}dy\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dv\,a^{\mathbf{E}}_{\pm,i}(v,\bar{\omega})\cdot(\pm\mathbf{E}^{l}\pm\hat{v}_{\pm}\times\mathbf{B}^{l}-m_{\pm}g\hat{e}_{3})\frac{f^{l+1}_{\pm}(t-|x-y|,\bar{y},v)}{|x-y|}\\
&\quad\mp\int_{B^{-}(x;t)}\frac{dy}{|y-x|}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dv\ \frac{\bar{\omega}+\hat{v}_{\pm}}{1+\hat{v}_{\pm}\cdot\bar{\omega}}\,\left(\pm\mathscr{E}\pm\hat{v}_{\pm}\times\mathscr{B}\right)\left(t-|x-y|,\bar{y}\right)\cdot\nabla_{v}F_{\pm,\textup{st}}(\bar{y},v)\\
&\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}(\mathscr{E}^{l+1})^{(2),\textup{acc}}_{\pm,iS}(t,x)+(\mathscr{E}^{l+1})^{(2),\textup{st}}_{\pm,iS}(t,x),\text{ with }\bar{z}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}(z_{1},z_{2},-z_{3})^{\top}.\end{split}

Here, the “acc”-term and “st”-term refer to the nonlinear contributions arising from the dynamical source and the stationary source, respectively. We emphasize that, in deriving the representation of the “st”-term, it is not necessary to perform the standard integration by parts with respect to the velocity derivative
\nabla_{v}
, since the required decay estimates for the momentum derivative of the stationary solution,
\nabla_{v}F_{\pm,\textup{st}}
, have already been established in (
2.12
).

In the following subsections, we will derive decay-in-time estimates for each of the above decomposed components of
\mathscr{E}^{l+1}
and
\mathscr{B}^{l+1}
.

5.2.1.
Decay Estimates for
\mathscr{E}^{l+1}_{\textup{hom},i}
and
\mathscr{B}^{l+1}_{\textup{hom},i}

Recall that, by (
A.3
), (
A.4
), (
3.34
), (
3.35
), and (
3.36
), the homogeneous components
\mathscr{E}^{l+1}_{\textup{hom},i}
and
\mathscr{B}^{l+1}_{\textup{hom},i}
have the following representation:

\begin{split}\mathscr{E}^{l+1}_{\textup{hom},i}(t,x)&=\frac{1}{4\pi t^{2}}\int_{\partial B(x;t)\cap\{y_{3}>0\}}\left(t\mathscr{E}^{1}_{0i}(y)+\mathscr{E}_{0i}(y)+\nabla\mathscr{E}_{0i}(y)\cdot(y-x)\right)dS_{y}\\
&\qquad-\frac{1}{4\pi t^{2}}\int_{\partial B(x;t)\cap\{y_{3}<0\}}\left(t\mathscr{E}^{1}_{0i}(\bar{y})+\mathscr{E}_{0i}(\bar{y})+\nabla\mathscr{E}_{0i}(\bar{y})\cdot(\bar{y}-\bar{x})\right)dS_{y},\text{ for }i=1,2,\\
\mathscr{E}^{l+1}_{\textup{hom},3}(t,x)&=\frac{1}{4\pi t^{2}}\int_{\partial B(x;t)\cap\{y_{3}>0\}}\left(t\mathscr{E}^{1}_{03}(y)+\mathscr{E}_{03}(y)+\nabla\mathscr{E}_{03}(y)\cdot(y-x)\right)dS_{y}\\
&\qquad+\frac{1}{4\pi t^{2}}\int_{\partial B(x;t)\cap\{y_{3}<0\}}\left(t\mathscr{E}^{1}_{03}(\bar{y})+\mathscr{E}_{03}(\bar{y})+\nabla\mathscr{E}_{03}(\bar{y})\cdot(\bar{y}-\bar{x})\right)dS_{y}\\
&\qquad-2\sum_{\iota=\pm}\iota\int_{B(x;t)\cap\{y_{3}=0\}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{f^{l+1}_{\iota}(t-|y-x|,y_{\parallel},0,v)}{|y-x|}dvdy_{\parallel},\\
\mathscr{B}^{l+1}_{\textup{hom},i}(t,x)&=\frac{1}{4\pi t^{2}}\int_{\partial B(x;t)\cap\{y_{3}>0\}}\left(t\mathscr{B}^{1}_{0i}(y)+\mathscr{B}_{0i}(y)+\nabla\mathscr{B}_{0i}(y)\cdot(y-x)\right)dS_{y}\\
&\qquad+\frac{1}{4\pi t^{2}}\int_{\partial B(x;t)\cap\{y_{3}<0\}}\left(t\mathscr{B}^{1}_{0i}(\bar{y})+\mathscr{B}_{0i}(\bar{y})+\nabla\mathscr{B}_{0i}(\bar{y})\cdot(\bar{y}-\bar{x})\right)dS_{y}\\
&\qquad+2\sum_{\pm}\pm\int_{B(x;t)\cap\{y_{3}=0\}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{\hat{v}_{j}f^{l+1}_{\pm}(t-|y-x|,y_{\parallel},0,v)}{|y-x|}dvdy_{\parallel},\text{ for }i,j=1,2\text{ with }j\neq i\\
\mathscr{B}^{l+1}_{\textup{hom},3}(t,x)&=\frac{1}{4\pi t^{2}}\int_{\partial B(x;t)\cap\{y_{3}>0\}}\left(t\mathscr{B}^{1}_{03}(y)+\mathscr{B}_{03}(y)+\nabla\mathscr{B}_{03}(y)\cdot(y-x)\right)dS_{y}\\
&\qquad-\frac{1}{4\pi t^{2}}\int_{\partial B(x;t)\cap\{y_{3}<0\}}\left(t\mathscr{B}^{1}_{03}(\bar{y})+\mathscr{B}_{03}(\bar{y})+\nabla\mathscr{B}_{03}(\bar{y})\cdot(\bar{y}-\bar{x})\right)dS_{y}.\end{split}

Without loss of generality, we make the decay estimates for the following integrals:

(5.11)

\frac{1}{4\pi t^{2}}\int_{\partial B(x;t)\cap\{y_{3}>0\}}\left(t\mathscr{B}^{1}_{01}(y)+\mathscr{B}_{01}(y)+\nabla\mathscr{B}_{01}(y)\cdot(y-x)\right)dS_{y},

and

(5.12)

\int_{B(x;t)\cap\{y_{3}=0\}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{f^{l+1}_{\pm}(t-|y-x|,y_{\parallel},0,v)}{|y-x|}dvdy_{\parallel},

since
|\hat{v}|\leq 1.

Indeed, the integral (
5.12
) has the same upper bound as that of
ib2
terms, whose decay estimate will be given in Section
5.2.3
. We omit it here.

Now, we establish a linear-in-time decay estimate for the integral (
5.11
). To this end, we assume that the initial data
\mathscr{B}^{1}_{01}(y)
and
\mathscr{B}_{01}(y)
(as well as the other components
\mathscr{B}^{1}_{0i}(y)
,
\mathscr{B}_{0i}(y)
,
\mathscr{E}^{1}_{0i}(y)
and
\mathscr{E}_{0i}(y)
for
i=1,2,3
) are compactly supported in the region
|y|\leq R_{0}
, for some
R_{0}>0
.
We perform the standard change of variables
y=x+t\omega
, where
\omega\in\mathbb{S}^{2}
. Then
y-x=t\omega
and
dS_{y}=t^{2}\,d\omega
, so the integral becomes

(5.13)

u(t,x)\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}\frac{1}{4\pi t^{2}}\int_{\partial B(x;t)\cap\{y_{3}>0\}}\left(t\,\mathscr{B}^{1}_{01}(y)+\mathscr{B}_{01}(y)+\nabla\mathscr{B}_{01}(y)\cdot(y-x)\right)dS_{y}\\
=\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\chi_{R_{0}}(x+t\omega)1_{\{(x+t\omega)_{3}>0\}}\left(t\,\mathscr{B}^{1}_{01}(x+t\omega)+\mathscr{B}_{01}(x+t\omega)+t\,\nabla\mathscr{B}_{01}(x+t\omega)\cdot\omega\right)d\omega,

where
\chi_{R_{0}}
denotes the characteristic function of the ball
B(0;R_{0})
.
Define

\Omega_{t}(x)\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}\left\{\omega\in\mathbb{S}^{2}:x+t\omega\in B(0;R_{0}),\ (x+t\omega)_{3}>0\right\}.

Then the above expression reduces to

\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\Omega_{t}(x)}\left(t\,\mathscr{B}^{1}_{01}(x+t\omega)+\mathscr{B}_{01}(x+t\omega)+t\,\nabla\mathscr{B}_{01}(x+t\omega)\cdot\omega\right)d\omega.

Next, let

M\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}\sup_{|z|\leq R_{0}}\left(|\mathscr{B}^{1}_{01}(z)|+|\mathscr{B}_{01}(z)|+|\nabla\mathscr{B}_{01}(z)|\right)<\infty,

so that the integrand is pointwise bounded by
M(1+t)
. Therefore, we first obtain a simple upper bound
M(1+t)
of
|u(t,x)|
in (
5.13
) for each
t>0
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}
, since
|\mathbb{S}^{2}|=4\pi
and
\chi_{R_{0}}1_{(x+t\omega)_{3}>}\leq 1.

It remains to estimate the surface measure of the integration domain
\Omega_{t}(x)
.
To this end, we observe that the condition
|x+t\omega|\leq R_{0}
defines a spherical cap on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
.
Fixing
x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
, the inequality

|x+t\omega|^{2}=|x|^{2}+2t\,x\cdot\omega+t^{2}\leq R_{0}^{2}

implies

-\frac{x}{|x|}\cdot\omega\geq\frac{t^{2}+|x|^{2}-R_{0}^{2}}{2t|x|}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}R(t,x).

Note that if
R(t,x)>1
, then there is no such
\omega\in\mathbb{S}^{2}
exists and hence
\Omega_{t}(x)
becomes empty. Therefore, we only consider the case that
R(t,x)\leq 1,
which provides another restriction that

(t-|x|)^{2}\leq R_{0}^{2}.

Namely, if
(t,x)
satisfies
(t-|x|)^{2}>R_{0}^{2},
then
|\Omega_{t}(x)|=0
and hence the integral (
5.13
) is zero. Now define the opening angle
\theta_{t,x}
of the spherical cap
\Omega_{t}(x)
. Note that the radius of the spherical cap has been normalized to
1.
Thus the surface area of the spherical cap is defined as

|\Omega_{t}(x)|=2\pi(1-\cos\theta_{t,x}),

and the opening angle
\theta_{t,x}
is defined through
\omega_{0}\in\mathbb{S}^{2}
which satisfies

\cos\theta_{t,x}=-\frac{x}{|x|}\cdot\omega_{0}=\min\{R(t,x),1\}.

Therefore, we have

|\Omega_{t}(x)|=2\pi(1-\cos\theta_{t,x})=2\pi\left(1+\frac{x}{|x|}\cdot\omega_{0}\right)=2\pi\left(1+\max\left\{-\frac{t^{2}+|x|^{2}-R_{0}^{2}}{2t|x|},-1\right\}\right)\\
=\max\left\{\pi\frac{R_{0}^{2}-(t-|x|)^{2}}{t|x|},0\right\}.

Combining the pointwise bound on the integrand and the measure of
\Omega_{t}(x)
, we obtain that the integral
u(t,x)
in (
5.13
) is bounded from above as

|u(t,x)|\leq\frac{1}{4\pi}M(1+t)\pi\max\left\{\frac{R_{0}^{2}-(t-|x|)^{2}}{t|x|},0\right\}\begin{cases}\lesssim\frac{M(1+t)}{t|x|},\text{ if }(t-|x|)^{2}\leq R_{0}^{2}.\\
=0,\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

Therefore, it suffices to consider the case
(t-|x|)^{2}\leq R_{0}^{2}
from now on, since the integral becomes trivial, otherwise.

We now split the case into two:
t>\frac{3}{2}R_{0}
and
t\leq\frac{3}{2}R_{0}.
If
t>\frac{3}{2}R_{0}
, then since

t-|x|\leq R_{0}
, we have

\frac{1}{|x|}\leq\frac{1}{t-R_{0}}.

Suppose
t=sR_{0}
for some
s>\frac{3}{2}.
Then

\frac{1}{t-R_{0}}=\frac{1}{(s-1)R_{0}}=\frac{s}{s-1}\frac{1}{t}\leq 3\frac{1}{t},

since
\frac{s}{s-1}<3
uniformly for any
s>\frac{3}{2}.
Furthermore, since
t>\frac{3}{2}R_{0},
we have

3\frac{1}{t}\leq\left(3+\frac{2}{R_{0}}\right)\frac{1}{1+t}.

Therefore, in this region, we have

|u(t,x)|\lesssim\frac{M(1+t)}{t(t-R_{0})}\lesssim\frac{3M(1+t)}{t^{2}}\lesssim\left(3+\frac{2}{R_{0}}\right)^{2}\frac{M}{3(1+t)},\text{ for }t>\frac{3}{2}R_{0}.

On the other hand, if
t\leq\frac{3}{2}R_{0}
, then note that
|u(t,x)|
in (
5.13
) is simply bounded from above by
M(1+t)
, and hence by
M(1+\frac{3}{2}R_{0})
.
Altogether, we obtain

(5.14)
