\lesssim C_{T}\langle\mathscr{V}^{l+1}_{\pm}(0)\rangle^{m}1_{t\leq t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}}|(\nabla_{v}F^{\textup{in}}_{\pm})(\mathscr{X}^{l+1}_{\pm}(0;t,x,v),\mathscr{V}^{l+1}_{\pm}(0;t,x,v))|\lesssim C_{T}\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\nabla_{v}F^{\textup{in}}_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}.

On the other hand, if
t>t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}},
by (
6.10
),

\displaystyle(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\tilde{\alpha}_{\pm}(t,x,v)1_{t>t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}}|(\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}G_{\pm})((x_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel},v_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})||\partial_{x_{3}}(x_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel}|

\displaystyle+(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\tilde{\alpha}_{\pm}(t,x,v)1_{t>t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}}|(\nabla_{v}G_{\pm})((x_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel},v_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})||\partial_{x_{3}}v_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}^{l+1}|

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{T}\bigg(1_{t>t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}}(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}|(\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}G_{\pm})((x_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel},v_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})|\tilde{\alpha}_{\pm}(t,x,v)\left|\frac{1}{|(\hat{\mathscr{V}}_{\pm}^{l+1})_{3}(t-t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}})|}+\frac{1}{\langle v\rangle}\right|

\displaystyle+1_{t>t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}}(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}|(\nabla_{v}G_{\pm})((x_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel},v_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})|\tilde{\alpha}_{\pm}(t,x,v)\left|\frac{1}{|(\hat{\mathscr{V}}_{\pm}^{l+1})_{3}(t-t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}})|}+1\right|\bigg),

where also used
t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}\leq T
for the terms with
1_{t>t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}}.
By using (
6.15
), (
6.16
) with
t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}\leq T
, and (
6.27
) with
s=t-t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}
for the terms with
1_{t>t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}},
we conclude that

\displaystyle\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\tilde{\alpha}_{\pm}\partial_{x_{3}}F^{l+1}_{\pm}(t,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{T}\bigg(\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m-1}\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}F^{\textup{in}}_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\tilde{\alpha}_{\pm}\partial_{x_{3}}F^{\textup{in}}_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\nabla_{v}F^{\textup{in}}_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\bigg)

\displaystyle+C_{T}\bigg(\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}G_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x_{\parallel},v}}+\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\nabla_{v}G_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x_{\parallel},v}}\bigg).

Finally, we consider a weighted upper-bound estimate for the momentum derivative
|\nabla_{v}F^{l+1}_{\pm}|.
By (
6.2
), we observe that
(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}|\nabla_{v}F^{l+1}_{\pm}|
is bounded from above by

\displaystyle(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}|\nabla_{v}F^{l+1}_{\pm}(t,x,v)|\leq(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\bigg|(\nabla_{x}F^{\textup{in}}_{\pm})(\mathscr{X}^{l+1}_{\pm}(0;t,x,v),\mathscr{V}^{l+1}_{\pm}(0;t,x,v))\cdot\nabla_{v}\mathscr{X}^{l+1}_{\pm}(0;t,x,v)

\displaystyle+(\nabla_{v}F^{\textup{in}}_{\pm})(\mathscr{X}^{l+1}_{\pm}(0;t,x,v),\mathscr{V}^{l+1}_{\pm}(0;t,x,v))\cdot\nabla_{v}\mathscr{V}^{l+1}_{\pm}(0;t,x,v)\bigg|1_{t\leq t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}(t,x,v)}

\displaystyle+(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\bigg|(\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}G_{\pm})((x_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel},v_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})\cdot\nabla_{v}(x_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel}+(\nabla_{v}G_{\pm})((x_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel},v_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})\cdot\nabla_{v}v_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}^{l+1}\bigg|1_{t>t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}(t,x,v)}

\displaystyle\lesssim 1_{t\leq t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}}(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}|(\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}F^{\textup{in}}_{\pm})(\mathscr{X}^{l+1}_{\pm}(0;t,x,v),\mathscr{V}^{l+1}_{\pm}(0;t,x,v))||\nabla_{v}(\mathscr{X}^{l+1}_{\pm})_{\parallel}(0;t,x,v)|

\displaystyle+1_{t\leq t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}}(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}|(\partial_{x_{3}}F^{\textup{in}}_{\pm})(\mathscr{X}^{l+1}_{\pm}(0;t,x,v),\mathscr{V}^{l+1}_{\pm}(0;t,x,v))||\nabla_{v}(\mathscr{X}^{l+1}_{\pm})_{3}(0;t,x,v)|

\displaystyle+1_{t\leq t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}}(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}|(\nabla_{v}F^{\textup{in}}_{\pm})(\mathscr{X}^{l+1}_{\pm}(0;t,x,v),\mathscr{V}^{l+1}_{\pm}(0;t,x,v))||\nabla_{v}\mathscr{V}^{l+1}_{\pm}(0;t,x,v)|

\displaystyle+1_{t>t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}}(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}|(\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}G_{\pm})((x_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel},v_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})||\nabla_{v}(x_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel}|+1_{t>t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}}(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}|(\nabla_{v}G_{\pm})((x_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel},v_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})||\nabla_{v}v_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}^{l+1}|.

Using (
6.27
) and (
6.8
) with
s=0
and (
6.20
) for
t\leq t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}
terms and using (
6.10
) for
t>t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}
terms, we obtain

\displaystyle(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}|\nabla_{v}F^{l+1}_{\pm}(t,x,v)|\lesssim C_{T}\bigg(\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m-1}\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}F^{\textup{in}}_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\tilde{\alpha}_{\pm}\partial_{x_{3}}F^{\textup{in}}_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\nabla_{v}F^{\textup{in}}_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\bigg)

\displaystyle+C_{T}(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m-1}\bigg(1_{t>t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}}|(\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}G_{\pm})((x_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel},v_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})|\frac{t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}}{|\hat{\mathscr{V}}_{\pm}^{l+1}(t-t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}})|}

\displaystyle+1_{t>t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}}|(\nabla_{v}G_{\pm})((x_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})_{\parallel},v_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}^{l+1})|\bigg|\frac{t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}}{|\hat{\mathscr{V}}_{\pm}^{l+1}(t-t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}})|(v^{0}_{\pm})}+1\bigg|\bigg),

where also used
t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}\leq T
for the terms with
1_{t>t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}}.

By using (
6.28
) and (
6.27
) with
s=t-t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}
for the terms with
1_{t>t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}},
we conclude that

\displaystyle\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\nabla_{v}F^{l+1}_{\pm}(t,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\lesssim C_{T}\bigg(\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m-1}\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}F^{\textup{in}}_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\tilde{\alpha}_{\pm}\partial_{x_{3}}F^{\textup{in}}_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\nabla_{v}F^{\textup{in}}_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\bigg)

\displaystyle+C_{T}\bigg(\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}G_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x_{\parallel},v}}+\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m-1}\nabla_{v}G_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x_{\parallel},v}}\bigg).

Lastly, we consider the temporal derivative
\partial_{t}F^{l+1}_{\pm}.
Using the Vlasov equation (
1.1
)
1
, we have

|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\partial_{t}F^{l+1}_{\pm}|\leq|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}F^{l+1}_{\pm}|+|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}(\hat{v}_{\pm})_{3}\partial_{x_{3}}F^{l+1}_{\pm}|+|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\nabla_{v}F^{l+1}_{\pm}||\mathscr{F}^{l}_{\pm}|.

Note that

(\hat{v}_{\pm})_{3}=\alpha_{\pm}(t,x_{\parallel},0,v)\leq 2\sqrt{\frac{\alpha_{\pm}^{2}(t,x_{\parallel},0,v)}{1+\alpha_{\pm}^{2}(t,x_{\parallel},0,v)}}\lesssim_{T}\tilde{\alpha}_{\pm}(s,\mathscr{X}^{l+1}_{\pm}(s),\mathscr{V}^{l+1}_{\pm}(s)),

for any
s\in(0,T).
Therefore, we conclude

\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\partial_{t}F^{l+1}_{\pm}(t,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\\
\lesssim_{T}\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}F^{l+1}_{\pm}(t,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\tilde{\alpha}_{\pm}\partial_{x_{3}}F^{l+1}_{\pm}(t,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\nabla_{v}F^{l+1}_{\pm}(t,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}.

This completes the proof of Proposition
6.9
.
∎

7.
Regularity Estimates for the Electromagnetic Fields

In this section, we provide consider derivative estimates of the self-consistent electromagnetic fields
(\mathbf{E}^{l+1},\mathbf{B}^{l+1})
whose representations are given via (
A.3
), (
A.1
), (
A.4
), and (
3.33
), and (
3.37
). In the following three subsections, we consider the derivatives of
(\mathbf{E}^{l+1},\mathbf{B}^{l+1})
in tangential, normal, and temporal directions and eventually prove the following proposition:

Proposition 7.1
.

Suppose that
\mathbf{E}^{l+1}
and
\mathbf{B}^{l+1}
are defined through (
A.1
), (
A.4
), (
3.33
), and (
3.37
). Suppose that
-(\mathscr{F}^{l}_{\pm})_{3}(t,x_{\parallel},0,v)>c_{0},
for some
c_{0}>0.
Let
g\geq 1
and
\beta>1
be chosen sufficiently large so that

\min\{m_{+}^{2},m_{-}^{2}\}g^{2}\beta\gg 1\quad\textup{and}\quad\min\{m_{+}^{2},m_{-}^{2}\}\beta^{4}\gg 1.

Also, suppose that the temporal derivatives of the initial profiles, understood through the system of equations, satisfy the assumptions (
2.14
)–(
2.15
). Then for any given
T>0
and some
m>4
, we have

(7.1)

\|(\mathbf{E}^{l+1},\mathbf{B}^{l+1})\|_{W^{1,\infty}_{t,x}([0,T]\times\Omega)}\lesssim_{T}(1+\|(\mathbf{E}^{\textup{in}},\mathbf{B}^{\textup{in}})\|_{C^{2}_{x}(\Omega)})(1+\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}F^{l+1}_{\pm}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t,x,v}([0,T]\times\Omega\times{\mathbb{R}^{3}})})\\
+\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}F^{l+1}_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x,v}([0,T]\times\Omega\times{\mathbb{R}^{3}})}+\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\tilde{\alpha}_{\pm}(t,x,v)\partial_{x_{3}}F^{l+1}_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x,v}([0,T]\times\Omega\times{\mathbb{R}^{3}})}.

Proof.

The proposition follows directly from Lemmas
7.2
,
7.3
, and
7.4
, which are established in the subsequent sections.
∎

7.1.
Normal Derivatives

We first introduce the estimate for normal derivatives.
We emphasize that these derivatives are controlled by tangential and temporal derivatives in conjunction with the governing Maxwell equations.
This represents a fundamentally different methodology from the traditional approach (cf.
[
5
]
).

Lemma 7.2
.

Suppose that
\mathbf{E}^{l+1}
and
\mathbf{B}^{l+1}
are defined through (
A.1
), (
A.4
), (
3.33
), and (
3.37
). Suppose that
-(\mathscr{F}^{l}_{\pm})_{3}(t,x_{\parallel},0,v)>c_{0},
for some
c_{0}>0.
Then for any given
T>0
and some
m>4
, we have

\hskip-8.53581pt\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\partial_{x_{3}}(\mathbf{E}^{l+1},\mathbf{B}^{l+1})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\lesssim_{T}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\|\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}(\mathbf{E}^{l+1},\mathbf{B}^{l+1})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+\|\partial_{t}(\mathbf{E}^{l+1}_{\parallel},\mathbf{B}^{l+1}_{\parallel})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}F^{l+1}_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\right).

Proof.

Using (
1.1
)
2
-(
1.1
)
5
, we obtain

\begin{split}\partial_{x_{3}}\mathbf{E}_{3}^{l+1}&=-\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}\cdot\mathbf{E}^{l+1}_{\parallel}+4\pi\rho^{l+1},\ \partial_{x_{3}}\mathbf{B}_{3}^{l+1}=-\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}\cdot\mathbf{B}^{l+1}_{\parallel},\\
\partial_{x_{3}}\mathbf{E}_{1}^{l+1}&=\partial_{x_{1}}\mathbf{E}_{3}^{l+1}-\partial_{t}\mathbf{B}_{2}^{l+1},\ \partial_{x_{3}}\mathbf{E}_{2}^{l+1}=\partial_{x_{2}}\mathbf{E}_{3}^{l+1}+\partial_{t}\mathbf{B}_{1}^{l+1},\\
\partial_{x_{3}}\mathbf{B}_{1}^{l+1}&=\partial_{x_{1}}\mathbf{B}_{3}^{l+1}+\partial_{t}\mathbf{E}_{2}^{l+1}+4\pi J_{2}^{l+1},\ \partial_{x_{3}}\mathbf{B}_{2}^{l+1}=\partial_{x_{2}}\mathbf{B}_{3}^{l+1}-\partial_{t}\mathbf{E}_{1}^{l+1}-4\pi J_{1}^{l+1}.\end{split}

Therefore, we obtain

\|\partial_{x_{3}}(\mathbf{E}^{l+1},\mathbf{B}^{l+1})\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\|\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}(\mathbf{E}^{l+1},\mathbf{B}^{l+1})\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial_{t}(\mathbf{E}^{l+1}_{\parallel},\mathbf{B}^{l+1}_{\parallel})\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\rho^{l+1}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|J^{l+1}\|_{L^{\infty}}\\
\lesssim\|\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}(\mathbf{E}^{l+1},\mathbf{B}^{l+1})\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial_{t}(\mathbf{E}^{l+1}_{\parallel},\mathbf{B}^{l+1}_{\parallel})\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}F^{l+1}_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}},

for
m>4
, since

(7.2)

|J^{l+1}(t,x)|\leq\rho^{l+1}(t,x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}F^{l+1}_{\pm}(t,x,v)dv\leq\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}F^{l+1}_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(v^{0}_{\pm})^{-m}dv\lesssim\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}F^{l+1}_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}.

Thus, we obtain the lemma by using the uniform estimates (
7.15
) and (
7.14
) on
\|\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}(\mathbf{E}^{l+1},\mathbf{B}^{l+1})\|_{L^{\infty}}
and
\|\partial_{t}(\mathbf{E}^{l+1}_{\parallel},\mathbf{B}^{l+1}_{\parallel})\|_{L^{\infty}}
, respectively.
∎

7.2.
Temporal Derivatives

This section is devoted to deriving uniform estimates on the temporal derivatives of the fields
(\mathbf{E}^{l+1},\mathbf{B}^{l+1})
. Special care is required due to the presence of temporal-physical boundaries at
t=0
and
x_{3}=0
. We study the system satisfied by
\partial_{t}\mathbf{E}^{l+1}
,
\partial_{t}\mathbf{B}^{l+1}
, and
\partial_{t}F^{l+1}_{\pm}
by formally differentiating in time the Vlasov–Maxwell system (
5.3
)–(
5.4
), the continuity equation (
1.3
), and the boundary conditions (
1.4
) and (
5.8
) at the sequential level
(l+1)
.
This represents a fundamentally different methodology from the traditional approach (cf.
[
5
]
).

Formally applying
\partial_{t}
yields the following system for
\partial_{t}\mathbf{E}^{l}
,
\partial_{t}\mathbf{B}^{l}
, and
\partial_{t}F^{l}_{\pm}
, which must be understood in the sense of distributions:

(7.3)

(\partial_{t}+\hat{v}_{\pm}\cdot\nabla_{x}+(\pm\mathbf{E}^{l}\pm\hat{v}_{\pm}\times\mathbf{B}^{l}-m_{\pm}g\hat{e}_{3})\cdot\nabla_{v})(\partial_{t}F^{l+1}_{\pm})=\mp(\partial_{t}\mathbf{E}^{l}+\hat{v}_{\pm}\times\partial_{t}\mathbf{B}^{l})\cdot\nabla_{v}F^{l+1}_{\pm},

(7.4)

\begin{split}\partial_{t}(\partial_{t}\mathbf{E}^{l})-\nabla_{x}\times(\partial_{t}\mathbf{B}^{l})&=-4\pi\partial_{t}J^{l},\ \partial_{t}(\partial_{t}\mathbf{B}^{l})+\nabla_{x}\times(\partial_{t}\mathbf{E}^{l})=0,\\
\nabla_{x}\cdot(\partial_{t}\mathbf{E}^{l})&=4\pi\partial_{t}\rho^{l},\ \nabla_{x}\cdot(\partial_{t}\mathbf{B}^{l})=0,\end{split}

and the differentiated continuity equation:

(7.5)

\partial_{t}(\partial_{t}\rho^{l+1})+\nabla_{x}\cdot(\partial_{t}J^{l+1})=0.

In addition, formally differentiating the boundary conditions yields, again in the sense of distributions:

(7.6)

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}\mathbf{E}_{1}^{l+1})\big|_{\partial\Omega}=0=(\partial_{t}\mathbf{E}_{2}^{l+1})\big|_{\partial\Omega},\quad(\partial_{t}\mathbf{B}_{3}^{l+1})\big|_{\partial\Omega}=0,

and the Neumann-type boundary conditions:

(7.7)

\partial_{x_{3}}(\partial_{t}\mathbf{E}_{3}^{l+1})=4\pi(\partial_{t}\rho^{l+1}),\quad\partial_{x_{3}}(\partial_{t}\mathbf{E}_{2}^{l+1})=-4\pi(\partial_{t}J^{l+1}_{1}),\quad\partial_{x_{3}}(\partial_{t}\mathbf{B}_{1}^{l+1})=4\pi(\partial_{t}J^{l+1}_{2}).

These boundary conditions are to be interpreted in the weak sense, meaning they hold through integration against test functions rather than pointwise evaluation. Accordingly, we ensure that
\partial_{t}\rho^{l+1}
and
\partial_{t}J^{l+1}
are controlled in
L^{\infty}
where these boundary relations make sense.

Finally, we prescribe the initial data for the temporal derivatives of the fields for each
i=1,2,3
:

(7.8)

(\partial_{t}\mathbf{E}_{i})(0,x)=\mathbf{E}^{1}_{0i}(x),\ (\partial_{t}^{2}\mathbf{E}_{i})(0,x)=\mathbf{E}^{2}_{0i}(x),\ (\partial_{t}\mathbf{B}_{i})(0,x)=\mathbf{B}^{1}_{0i}(x),\ (\partial_{t}^{2}\mathbf{B}_{i})(0,x)=\mathbf{B}^{2}_{0i}(x).

The initial temporal derivatives
\partial_{t}\mathbf{E}^{l}(0,x)
and
\partial_{t}\mathbf{B}^{l}(0,x)
are determined from the initial data

(\mathbf{E}^{l}(0,x),\mathbf{B}^{l}(0,x),\rho(0,x),J(0,x))
via the Maxwell equations evaluated at
t=0
. Similarly,
\partial_{t}^{2}\mathbf{E}^{l}(0,x)
and
\partial_{t}^{2}\mathbf{B}^{l}(0,x)
are obtained by differentiating the system once in time. All initial values are understood in the distributional sense.

Given the decay estimates for the momentum derivatives (
6.24
) for
\nabla_{v}F^{l+1}_{\pm}
, we provide decay estimates for the temporal derivative
\partial_{t}F^{l+1}_{\pm}
of the distribution
F^{l+1}_{\pm}
and uniform estimates on
\partial_{t}\mathbf{E}^{l+1}
and
\partial_{t}\mathbf{B}^{l+1}
via a bootstrap argument. For a bootstrap argument, we make the following bootstrap assumptions on
\partial_{t}F^{l}_{\pm}
,
\partial_{t}\mathbf{E}^{l}
and
\partial_{t}\mathbf{B}^{l}
. In the case when
\Omega=\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}
, let
\partial_{t}F^{l}_{\pm},\partial_{t}\mathbf{E}^{l}
, and
\partial_{t}\mathbf{B}^{l}

satisfy

(7.9)

\sup_{t\geq 0}\left\|e^{\frac{\beta}{2}|x_{\parallel}|}e^{\frac{\beta}{4}(v^{0}_{\pm}+m_{\pm}gx_{3})}\partial_{t}F^{l}_{\pm}(t,\cdot,\cdot)\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\bigg(\|\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}\partial_{t}F_{\pm}(0,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\\
+\frac{64CD_{0}}{5\beta e}\left(\|\mathrm{w}^{2}_{\pm,\beta}(x,v)\nabla_{x,v}F^{\textup{in}}_{\pm}(x,v)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+\|\mathrm{w}^{2}_{\pm,\beta}(x_{\parallel},0,v)\nabla_{x_{\parallel},v}G_{\pm}(x_{\parallel},v)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x_{\parallel},v}}\right)\bigg),

and

(7.10)

\sup_{t\geq 0}\|(\partial_{t}\mathbf{E}^{l},\partial_{t}\mathbf{B}^{l})\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq D_{0}\min\{m_{-},m_{+}\}g,

for some uniform constant
D_{0}>0
where
C>0
is the same constant as that of (
6.24
) and the weight
\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}
is defined as (
3.46
). Note that this constant
D_{0}
can be sufficiently large.

In the following subsections, we will prove that the bootstrap ansatz (
7.9
) and (
7.10
) hold also on the sequential level of
(l+1)
given the momentum derivative estimate (
6.24
).

7.2.1.
Estimates for
\partial_{t}F^{l+1}_{\pm}
for
\Omega=\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}

We first prove that (
7.9
) holds for
F^{l+1}_{\pm}
. Since
\partial_{t}F^{l+1}_{\pm}
satisfies (
7.3
), we can write
\partial_{t}F^{l+1}_{\pm}
in the mild form as

(7.11)

\partial_{t}F^{l+1}_{\pm}(t,x,v)=1_{t\leq t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}(t,x,v)}\partial_{t}F_{\pm}(0,\mathscr{X}^{l+1}_{\pm}(0;t,x,v),\mathscr{V}^{l+1}_{\pm}(0;t,x,v))\\
\mp\int^{t}_{\max\{0,t-t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}\}}\left(\partial_{t}\mathbf{E}^{l}(s,\mathscr{X}^{l+1}_{\pm}(s))+\hat{\mathscr{V}}^{l+1}_{\pm}(s)\times\partial_{t}\mathbf{B}^{l}(s,\mathscr{X}^{l+1}_{\pm}(s))\right)\cdot\nabla_{v}F^{l+1}_{\pm}(s,\mathscr{X}^{l+1}_{\pm}(s),\mathscr{V}^{l+1}_{\pm}(s))ds.

Given that
\|\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}\nabla_{v}F^{l+1}_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}}
is bounded (see (
6.24
)), by (
7.10
), (
7.11
) and that
|\hat{\mathscr{V}}^{l+1}_{\pm}|\leq 1
, we obtain that

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}F^{l+1}_{\pm}(t,x,v)|

\displaystyle\leq 1_{t\leq t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}(t,x,v)}|\partial_{t}F_{\pm}(0,\mathscr{X}^{l+1}_{\pm}(0;t,x,v),\mathscr{V}^{l+1}_{\pm}(0;t,x,v))|

\displaystyle+1_{t\leq t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}(t,x,v)}\int^{t}_{0}\ D_{0}\min\{m_{-},m_{+}\}g|\nabla_{v}F^{l+1}_{\pm}(s,\mathscr{X}^{l+1}_{\pm}(s),\mathscr{V}^{l+1}_{\pm}(s))|ds

\displaystyle+1_{t>t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}(t,x,v)}\int^{t}_{t-t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}}D_{0}\min\{m_{-},m_{+}\}g|\nabla_{v}F^{l+1}_{\pm}(s,\mathscr{X}^{l+1}_{\pm}(s),\mathscr{V}^{l+1}_{\pm}(s))|ds

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1_{t\leq t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}(t,x,v)}}{\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}(\mathscr{Z}^{l+1}_{\pm}(0;t,x,v))}\|\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}\partial_{t}F_{\pm}(0,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle+1_{t\leq t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}(t,x,v)}D_{0}\min\{m_{-},m_{+}\}g\|\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}\nabla_{v}F^{l+1}_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x,v}}\int^{t}_{0}\frac{1}{\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}(\mathscr{Z}^{l+1}_{\pm}(s;t,x,v))}ds

\displaystyle+1_{t>t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}(t,x,v)}D_{0}\min\{m_{-},m_{+}\}g\|\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}\nabla_{v}F^{l+1}_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x,v}}\int^{t}_{t-t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}}\frac{1}{\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}(\mathscr{Z}^{l+1}_{\pm}(s;t,x,v))}ds.

Using (
3.56
) and (
3.58
), we further have

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}F^{l+1}_{\pm}(t,x,v)|

\displaystyle\leq 1_{t\leq t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}(t,x,v)}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\beta v_{\pm}^{0}-\frac{1}{2}m_{\pm}g\beta x_{3}-\frac{\beta}{2}|x_{\parallel}|}\|\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}\partial_{t}F_{\pm}(0,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle+1_{t\leq t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}(t,x,v)}D_{0}\min\{m_{-},m_{+}\}g\|\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}\nabla_{v}F^{l+1}_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x,v}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\beta v_{\pm}^{0}-\frac{1}{2}m_{\pm}g\beta x_{3}-\frac{\beta}{2}|x_{\parallel}|}t

\displaystyle+1_{t>t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}(t,x,v)}D_{0}\min\{m_{-},m_{+}\}g\|\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}\nabla_{v}F^{l+1}_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x,v}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\beta v_{\pm}^{0}-\frac{1}{2}m_{\pm}g\beta x_{3}-\frac{\beta}{2}|x_{\parallel}|}t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\frac{1}{2}\beta v_{\pm}^{0}-\frac{1}{2}m_{\pm}g\beta x_{3}-\frac{\beta}{2}|x_{\parallel}|}\|\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}\partial_{t}F_{\pm}(0,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle+D_{0}\min\{m_{-},m_{+}\}g\|\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}\nabla_{v}F^{l+1}_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x,v}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\beta v_{\pm}^{0}-\frac{1}{2}m_{\pm}g\beta x_{3}-\frac{\beta}{2}|x_{\parallel}|}t^{l+1}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\frac{1}{2}\beta v_{\pm}^{0}-\frac{1}{2}m_{\pm}g\beta x_{3}-\frac{\beta}{2}|x_{\parallel}|}\|\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}\partial_{t}F_{\pm}(0,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle+\frac{16D_{0}}{5}(v_{\pm}^{0}+m_{\pm}g\beta x_{3})\|\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}\nabla_{v}F^{l+1}_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x,v}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\beta v_{\pm}^{0}-\frac{1}{2}m_{\pm}g\beta x_{3}-\frac{\beta}{2}|x_{\parallel}|}

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\frac{1}{2}\beta v_{\pm}^{0}-\frac{1}{2}m_{\pm}g\beta x_{3}-\frac{\beta}{2}|x_{\parallel}|}\|\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}\partial_{t}F_{\pm}(0,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle+\frac{64D_{0}}{5\beta e}\|\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}\nabla_{v}F^{l+1}_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x,v}}e^{-\frac{1}{4}\beta v_{\pm}^{0}-\frac{1}{4}m_{\pm}g\beta x_{3}-\frac{\beta}{2}|x_{\parallel}|},

where the last inequality is by the inequality that
xe^{-\frac{\beta}{2}x}\leq\frac{4}{\beta e}e^{-\frac{\beta}{4}x}.

Therefore, by (
6.24
), we conclude

(7.12)

\sup_{t\geq 0}\left\|e^{\frac{\beta}{2}|x_{\parallel}|}e^{\frac{\beta}{4}(v^{0}_{\pm}+m_{\pm}gx_{3})}\partial_{t}F^{l+1}_{\pm}(t,\cdot,\cdot)\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\bigg(\|\mathrm{w}_{\pm,\beta}\partial_{t}F_{\pm}(0,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\\
+\frac{64CD_{0}}{5\beta e}\left(\|\mathrm{w}^{2}_{\pm,\beta}(x,v)\nabla_{x,v}F^{\textup{in}}_{\pm}(x,v)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+\|\mathrm{w}^{2}_{\pm,\beta}(x_{\parallel},0,v)\nabla_{x_{\parallel},v}G_{\pm}(x_{\parallel},v)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x_{\parallel},v}}\right)\bigg).

This completes the decay estimate for the temporal derivative
\partial_{t}F^{l+1}_{\pm}.

7.2.2.
Estimates for
\partial_{t}\mathbf{E}^{l+1}
and
\partial_{t}\mathbf{B}^{l+1}

Given (
7.10
) and (
6.24
), we now prove that (
7.10
) also holds for
\partial_{t}\mathbf{E}^{l+1}
and
\partial_{t}\mathbf{B}^{l+1}
.
Notice that the system (
7.3
)–(
7.4
) has the same structure with the system of (
5.3
)–(
5.4
) if we translate the notations in (
7.3
)–(
7.4
) as follows:

(7.13)

\begin{split}\partial_{t}F^{l+1}_{\pm}&\mapsto f^{l+1}_{\pm},\ \partial_{t}\mathbf{E}^{l}\mapsto\mathscr{E}^{l},\ \partial_{t}\mathbf{B}^{l}\mapsto\mathscr{B}^{l},\ \nabla_{v}F^{l+1}_{\pm}\mapsto\nabla_{v}F_{\pm,\textup{st}},\ \partial_{t}\rho^{l}\mapsto\rho^{l}_{\textup{pert}},\ \partial_{t}J^{l}\mapsto J^{l}_{\textup{pert}}.\end{split}

Note that the structure of the continuity equation and the initial-boundary conditions (
7.5
)–(
7.8
) are also the same under the translation of the notations. Therefore, we do not need to repeat the uniform estimates for
\partial_{t}\mathbf{E}^{l+1}
, and
\partial_{t}\mathbf{B}^{l+1}
given that

(1)

\partial_{t}F^{l+1}_{\pm}
satisfies the same upper-bound estimate for
f^{l+1}_{\pm}
in (
5.10
),

(2)

\partial_{t}\mathbf{E}^{l}
and
\partial_{t}\mathbf{B}^{l}
satisfy the same bootstrap ansatz for
\mathscr{E}^{l}
and
\mathscr{B}^{l}
in (
5.7
),

(3)

\nabla_{v}F^{l+1}_{\pm}
satisfies the same upper-bound estimate for
\nabla_{v}F_{\pm,\textup{st}}
in (
4.33
).

Indeed, all of the necessary conditions for the temporal derivative estimates are already satisfied by the decay estimates (
6.24
) and (
7.12
), together with the bootstrap ansatz (
7.10
). The only difference compared to the bootstrap ansatz (
5.7
) for
\mathscr{E}^{l}
and
\mathscr{B}^{l}
lies in the constant coefficient: in (
7.10
), the constant is
D_{0}
instead of
\frac{1}{16}
for the previous estimate on
\mathbf{E}^{l}
and
\mathbf{B}^{l}
via (
5.7
) and (
4.11
).

This difference, however, does not create any additional difficulty. Throughout the analysis, we continue to follow the same characteristic trajectory
(\mathscr{X}^{l+1}_{\pm},\mathscr{V}^{l+1}_{\pm})
, which is based on the fields
(\mathbf{E}^{l},\mathbf{B}^{l})
and not on their temporal derivatives
(\partial_{t}\mathbf{E}^{l},\partial_{t}\mathbf{B}^{l})
. Since we already have the uniform bound (
4.18
) and (
5.7
) for
(\mathbf{E}^{l},\mathbf{B}^{l})
, the characteristic trajectories
(\mathscr{X}^{l+1}_{\pm},\mathscr{V}^{l+1}_{\pm})
remain well-controlled. In particular, the weight comparison argument (
3.58
) used in the proof of (
7.12
) remains valid.

In the uniform estimate for
\partial_{t}\mathbf{E}^{l}
and
\partial_{t}\mathbf{B}^{l}
, the main new feature is the nonlinear terms
\partial_{t}\mathbf{E}^{l}_{S}
and
\partial_{t}\mathbf{B}^{l}_{S}
, which now involve the larger constant
D_{0}
rather than
\frac{1}{8}
. However, thanks to the additional factor of
\frac{1}{\beta^{4}}
in the coefficient
c_{\pm,\beta}
appearing in (
5.34
) and (
5.35
), we can absorb this difference by choosing
\beta
sufficiently large. Specifically, the final estimates remain sufficiently small to close the bootstrap for (
7.10
).
Therefore, by following the same proof strategy as in Section
5
, but adapted with the new notations introduced in (
7.13
), we consequently obtain the following lemma:

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
g\geq 1
and
\beta>1
be chosen sufficiently large so that

\min\{m_{+}^{2},m_{-}^{2}\}g^{2}\beta\gg 1\quad\textup{and}\quad\min\{m_{+}^{2},m_{-}^{2}\}\beta^{4}\gg 1.

Also, suppose that the temporal derivatives of the initial profiles, understood through the system of equations, satisfy the assumptions (
2.14
)–(
2.15
).
Then the uniform upper bound

(7.14)

\sup_{t\geq 0}\|(\partial_{t}\mathbf{E}^{l+1},\partial_{t}\mathbf{B}^{l+1})\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq D_{0}\min\{m_{+},m_{-}\}g

holds for the temporal derivatives.

Lastly, we introduce the following lemma on the tangential derivatives.

Lemma 7.4

(Tangential derivatives)
.

Suppose that
\mathbf{E}^{l+1}
and
\mathbf{B}^{l+1}
are defined through (
A.1
), (
A.4
), (
3.33
), and (
3.37
). Suppose that
-(\mathscr{F}^{l}_{\pm})_{3}(t,x_{\parallel},0,v)>c_{0},
for some
c_{0}>0.
For some
T>0
and
m>4,
the following estimates hold:

(7.15)

\|\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}\mathbf{E}^{l+1}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}\mathbf{B}^{l+1}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim_{T,m_{\pm},m,g}(1+\|(\mathbf{E}^{\textup{in}},\mathbf{B}^{\textup{in}})\|_{C^{2}_{x}(\Omega)})(1+\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}F^{l+1}_{\pm}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t,x,v}([0,T]\times\Omega\times{\mathbb{R}^{3}})})\\
+\|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{m}\nabla_{x_{\parallel}}F^{l+1}_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x,v}([0,T]\times\Omega\times{\mathbb{R}^{3}})}.

Proof.

Given the derivative estimates on the trajectories and the velocity distribution obtained in Section
6
above, Lemma
7.4
on the tangential derivatives is proved in the same manner of
[
5
, Lemma 7, Eq. (3.2)]
. We omit the proof for the sake of simplicity.
∎

Remark 7.5
.

All the derivative estimates made in this section are uniform in
l
by the additional estimate (
6.26
) on the derivatives of
F_{\pm}
. Thus, the limit
(\mathbf{E}^{\infty},\mathbf{B}^{\infty})
also satisfies the same estimate.

8.
Global Existence

In this section, we finally provide the proof of the existence and uniqueness of solutions to the dynamical Vlasov–Maxwell systems.

8.1.
Global Existence and Regularity

We now prove the global-in-time existence of solutions for the dynamical problems on the Vlasov–Maxwell system (
1.1
). In both cases, we consider the iterated sequences of perturbations
(f^{l}_{\pm},\mathscr{E}^{l},\mathscr{B}^{l})
to the linear systems (
5.3
)-(
5.4
). Both linear systems admit solutions
(F^{l}_{\pm},\mathbf{E}^{l},\mathbf{B}^{l})
and
(f^{l}_{\pm},\mathscr{E}^{l},\mathscr{B}^{l})
for each
l\geq 0
due to the hyperbolicity of the operators.
In order to pass to the limit as
l\to\infty
and to prove that these limits actually solve the original nonlinear Vlasov–Maxwell system (
1.1
) in the weak sense, we have to pass to the limit of all the linear and nonlinear terms appearing in the iterated system (
5.3
)-(
5.4
). To this end, we will additionally prove here that
F_{\pm}^{l}
and
f_{\pm}^{l}
are indeed Cauchy so that
F_{\pm}^{l}\to F^{\infty}_{\pm}
and
f_{\pm}^{l}\to f^{\infty}_{\pm}
strongly as
l\to\infty
. We introduce the following propositions on the Cauchy property of the sequences.

Proposition 8.1
.

For each fixed
(t,x,v)\in(0,T)\times(\bar{\Omega}\times{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\setminus\gamma_{0})
,
(F_{\pm}^{l}(t,x,v))_{l\in\mathbb{N}}
and
(f_{\pm}^{l}(t,x,v))_{l\in\mathbb{N}}
are Cauchy.

Remark 8.2
.

The decay estimate for the momentum derivatives
\nabla_{v}F_{\pm}
(Proposition
6.8
) plays a crucial role in this proof below.

Proof.

Since the perturbation
f_{\pm}^{l}
can also be written as
F_{\pm}^{l}-F_{\pm,\textup{st}}
for the steady-state
F_{\pm,\textup{st}}
with Jüttner-Maxwell upper bound solving (
2.4
), it suffices to prove the Cauchy property for
(F_{\pm}^{l}(t,x,v))_{l\in\mathbb{N}}
. Fix
N_{0}\in\mathbb{N}.
Then for any
k,n\geq N_{0}
integers with
k\geq n
, we have

(8.1)

\begin{split}(&F^{k}_{\pm}-F^{n}_{\pm})(0,x,v)=0,\ (F^{k}_{\pm}-F^{n}_{\pm})(t,x_{\parallel},0,v)|_{\gamma_{-}}=0,\end{split}

and

\partial_{t}(F^{k}_{\pm}-F^{n}_{\pm})+(\hat{v}_{\pm})\cdot\nabla_{x}(F^{k}_{\pm}-F^{n}_{\pm})+\left(\pm\mathbf{E}^{k-1}\pm(\hat{v}_{\pm})\times\mathbf{B}^{k-1}-m_{\pm}g\hat{e}_{3}\right)\cdot\nabla_{v}(F^{k}_{\pm}-F^{n}_{\pm})\\
=-\left(\pm(\mathbf{E}^{k-1}-\mathbf{E}^{n-1})\pm(\hat{v}_{\pm})\times(\mathbf{B}^{k-1}-\mathbf{B}^{n-1})\right)\cdot\nabla_{v}F^{n}_{\pm}.

By (
8.1
), we have

(8.2)

(F^{k}_{\pm}-F^{n}_{\pm})(t,x,v)=\mp\int^{t}_{\max\{0,t-t^{k}_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}\}}\left((\mathbf{E}^{k-1}-\mathbf{E}^{n-1})(s,\mathscr{X}^{k}_{\pm}(s))+\hat{\mathscr{V}}^{k}_{\pm}(s)\times(\mathbf{B}^{k-1}-\mathbf{B}^{n-1})(s,\mathscr{X}^{k}_{\pm}(s))\right)\\
\cdot\nabla_{v}F^{n}_{\pm}(s,\mathscr{X}^{k}_{\pm}(s),\mathscr{V}^{k}_{\pm}(s))ds,

using the iterated characteristic trajectories (
5.5
). Here, note that
(\mathbf{E}^{k-1},\mathbf{B}^{k-1})
and
(\mathbf{E}^{n-1},\mathbf{B}^{n-1})
solve the iterated Maxwell equations under the same initial data, we have zero initial conditions for the difference
(\mathbf{E}^{k-1}-\mathbf{E}^{n-1},\mathbf{B}^{k-1}-\mathbf{B}^{n-1})
. Therefore, using the energy comparison that

(8.3)

(v^{0}_{\pm})\lesssim\langle\mathscr{V}^{l+1}_{\pm}(s)\rangle+\bigg|\int_{s}^{t}d\tau\ \mathscr{F}^{l}_{\pm}(\tau,\mathscr{X}^{l+1}_{\pm}(\tau),\mathscr{V}^{l+1}_{\pm}(\tau))\bigg|\lesssim\langle\mathscr{V}^{l+1}_{\pm}(s)\rangle+C_{2}T\lesssim C_{T}\langle\mathscr{V}^{l+1}_{\pm}(s)\rangle,

given by (
6.25
), we obtain for some positive
\delta\in(0,1)
,

(8.4)

\begin{split}&|((v_{\pm}^{0})^{4+\delta}(F^{k}_{\pm}-F^{n}_{\pm}))(t,x,v)|\\
&\leq C_{t}\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\|((v_{\pm}^{0})^{4+\delta}\nabla_{v}F^{n}_{\pm})(t,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\int_{0}^{t}\left(\|(\mathbf{E}^{k-1}-\mathbf{E}^{n-1})(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|(\mathbf{B}^{k-1}-\mathbf{B}^{n-1})(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\right)ds\\
&\leq C^{\prime}_{t}\int_{0}^{t}\left(\|(\mathbf{E}^{k-1}-\mathbf{E}^{n-1})(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|(\mathbf{B}^{k-1}-\mathbf{B}^{n-1})(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\right)ds,\end{split}

via the derivative upper bound estimate (
6.26
) for every sequence element
F^{n}_{\pm}
. Now we make estimates on each decomposed piece of

(\mathbf{E}^{k-1}-\mathbf{E}^{n-1},\mathbf{B}^{k-1}-\mathbf{B}^{n-1})
using the representations (
A.1
), (
A.4
), (
3.33
), and (
3.37
).

First of all, note that the differences
(\mathbf{E}^{k-1}-\mathbf{E}^{n-1},\mathbf{B}^{k-1}-\mathbf{B}^{n-1})
have zero homogeneous terms in their representations since
\mathbf{E}^{m-1}_{\textup{hom}}=\mathbf{E}^{n-1}_{\textup{hom}}
and
\mathbf{B}^{m-1}_{\textup{hom}}=\mathbf{B}^{n-1}_{\textup{hom}}
.

Regarding the
b2
boundary terms, we observe that for
i=1,2
and
3
,

(8.5)

|(\mathbf{E}^{k-1}-\mathbf{E}^{n-1})^{(1)}_{\pm,ib2}(t,x)|+|(\mathbf{E}^{k-1}-\mathbf{E}^{n-1})^{(2)}_{\pm,ib2}(t,x)|\\
\leq 2\int_{B(x;t)\cap\{y_{3}=0\}}\frac{dy_{\parallel}}{|y-x|}\int_{v_{3}\leq 0}dv\ \left(1+|(\hat{v}_{\pm})_{3}|\frac{\sqrt{m_{\pm}^{2}+|v|^{2}}}{m_{\pm}}\right)|(F^{k-1}_{\pm}-F^{n-1}_{\pm})(t-|x-y|,y_{\parallel},0,v)|,

by (
5.32
), since
(F^{k-1}_{\pm}-F^{n-1}_{\pm})(t,x_{\parallel},0,v)|_{\gamma_{-}}=0.
Then by further making the changes of variables
y_{\parallel}\mapsto z\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}y_{\parallel}-x_{\parallel}
and then
z\mapsto(r,\theta)
with
|z|=r,
we have

|(\mathbf{E}^{k-1}-\mathbf{E}^{n-1})^{(1)}_{\pm,ib2}(t,x)|+|(\mathbf{E}^{k-1}-\mathbf{E}^{n-1})^{(2)}_{\pm,ib2}(t,x)|\\
\leq\frac{8\pi}{m_{\pm}}\int_{0}^{\sqrt{t^{2}-x_{3}^{2}}}dr\ \frac{r}{\sqrt{r^{2}+x_{3}^{2}}}\left\|((v^{0}_{\pm})^{4+\delta}(F^{k-1}_{\pm}-F^{n-1}_{\pm}))\left(t-\sqrt{r^{2}+x_{3}^{2}},\cdot,\cdot\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\int_{v_{3}\leq 0}dv\ (v^{0}_{\pm})^{-3-\delta},

for any fixed
\delta>0
. By further making the change of variables
r\mapsto\tau\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}t-\sqrt{r^{2}+x_{3}^{2}},
we have

\hskip-5.69054pt|(\mathbf{E}^{k-1}-\mathbf{E}^{n-1})^{(1)}_{\pm,ib2}(t,x)|+|(\mathbf{E}^{k-1}-\mathbf{E}^{n-1})^{(2)}_{\pm,ib2}(t,x)|\lesssim\frac{1}{m_{\pm}}\int_{0}^{t-x_{3}}d\tau\ \left\|((v^{0}_{\pm})^{4+\delta}(F^{k-1}_{\pm}-F^{n-1}_{\pm}))\left(\tau,\cdot,\cdot\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}.

Note that the
b1
initial-value parts in the Glassey-Strauss representations depends only on the initial difference
(F^{k-1}_{\pm}-F^{n-1}_{\pm})(0,x,v)
which is zero. Therefore, all the
b1
terms in the representations of
(\mathbf{E}^{k-1}-\mathbf{E}^{n-1},\mathbf{B}^{k-1}-\mathbf{B}^{n-1})
are zero.

Regarding the
T
terms, we observe that by the representation (
A.1
)-(
A.4
), and the kernel estimate (
5.26
), we have

(8.6)

|(\mathbf{E}^{k-1}-\mathbf{E}^{n-1})^{(1)}_{\pm,T}(t,x)|\lesssim\int_{B^{+}(x;t)}\frac{dy}{|y-x|^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dv\ \frac{v^{0}_{\pm}}{m_{\pm}}(F^{k-1}_{\pm}-F^{n-1}_{\pm})(t-|x-y|,y,v)\\
+2\int_{B(x;t)\cap\{y_{3}=0\}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{(F^{k-1}_{\pm}-F^{n-1}_{\pm})(t-|y-x|,y_{\parallel},0,v)}{|y-x|}dvdy_{\parallel}.

Note that

\displaystyle\int_{B^{+}(x;t)}\frac{dy}{|y-x|^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dv\ \frac{v^{0}_{\pm}}{m_{\pm}}(F^{k-1}_{\pm}-F^{n-1}_{\pm})(t-|x-y|,y,v)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B^{+}(x;t)}\frac{dy}{|y-x|^{2}}\left\|((v^{0}_{\pm})^{4+\delta}(F^{k-1}_{\pm}-F^{n-1}_{\pm}))\left(t-|x-y|,y,\cdot\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{v}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dv\ \frac{(v^{0}_{\pm})^{-3-\delta}}{m_{\pm}}

\displaystyle\leq 4\pi C_{m_{\pm},\delta}\int_{0}^{t}dr\ \left\|((v^{0}_{\pm})^{4+\delta}(F^{k-1}_{\pm}-F^{n-1}_{\pm}))\left(t-r,\cdot,\cdot\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle=4\pi C_{m_{\pm},\delta}\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\ \left\|((v^{0}_{\pm})^{4+\delta}(F^{k-1}_{\pm}-F^{n-1}_{\pm}))\left(\tau,\cdot,\cdot\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}},

where we made the changes of variables
y\mapsto y-x=r\omega
with
r\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}|y-x|
and
\omega\in\mathbb{S}^{2}
, and then
r\mapsto\tau\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}t-r.
On the other hand, we also note that

(8.7)

\begin{split}&2\int_{B(x;t)\cap\{y_{3}=0\}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{(F^{k-1}_{\pm}-F^{n-1}_{\pm})(t-|y-x|,y_{\parallel},0,v)}{|y-x|}dvdy_{\parallel}\\
&\leq 2\int_{B(x;t)\cap\{y_{3}=0\}}\frac{dy_{\parallel}}{|y-x|}\left\|((v^{0}_{\pm})^{4+\delta}(F^{k-1}_{\pm}-F^{n-1}_{\pm}))\left(t-|x-y|,y,\cdot\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{v}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dv\ \frac{(v^{0}_{\pm})^{-3-\delta}}{m_{\pm}}\\
&\leq 4\pi C_{m_{\pm},\delta}\int_{0}^{t-x_{3}}d\tau\ \left\|((v^{0}_{\pm})^{4+\delta}(F^{k-1}_{\pm}-F^{n-1}_{\pm}))\left(\tau,\cdot,\cdot\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}},\end{split}

by further making the changes of variables
y_{\parallel}\mapsto z\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}y_{\parallel}-x_{\parallel}
, then
z\mapsto(r,\theta)
with
|z|=r
and
\theta\in[0,2\pi],
and finally
r\mapsto\tau\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}t-\sqrt{r^{2}+x_{3}^{2}}.
Altogether, we have

|(\mathbf{E}^{k-1}-\mathbf{E}^{n-1})^{(1)}_{\pm,T}(t,x)|\lesssim C_{m_{\pm},\delta}\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\ \left\|((v^{0}_{\pm})^{4+\delta}(F^{k-1}_{\pm}-F^{n-1}_{\pm}))\left(\tau,\cdot,\cdot\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}.

Similarly, we have the same upper bound for
|(\mathbf{E}^{k-1}-\mathbf{E}^{n-1})^{(2)}_{\pm,T}(t,x)|
. Regarding the difference in magnetic fields
(\mathbf{B}^{k-1}-\mathbf{B}^{n-1})_{\pm,T}
we instead use the kernel estimate (
5.39
), and obtain the same upper bound. Thus we conclude that

(8.8)

|(\mathbf{E}^{k-1}-\mathbf{E}^{n-1})_{\pm,T}(t,x)|+|(\mathbf{B}^{k-1}-\mathbf{B}^{n-1})_{\pm,T}(t,x)|\\
\lesssim C_{m_{\pm},\delta}\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\ \left\|((v^{0}_{\pm})^{4+\delta}(F^{k-1}_{\pm}-F^{n-1}_{\pm}))\left(\tau,\cdot,\cdot\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}.

Regarding the nonlinear
S
terms, the integrands in the differences
(\mathbf{E}^{k-1}-\mathbf{E}^{n-1})_{\pm,S}
and
(\mathbf{B}^{k-1}-\mathbf{B}^{n-1})_{\pm,S}
involve the following difference by the representations in (
A.1
), (
A.4
), (
3.33
), and (
3.37
):

(\pm\mathbf{E}^{k-2}\pm\hat{v}_{\pm}\times\mathbf{B}^{k-2}-m_{\pm}g\hat{e}_{3})F^{k-1}_{\pm}-(\pm\mathbf{E}^{n-2}\pm\hat{v}_{\pm}\times\mathbf{B}^{n-2}-m_{\pm}g\hat{e}_{3})F^{n-1}_{\pm}.

We further write this as

(\pm\mathbf{E}^{k-2}\pm\hat{v}_{\pm}\times\mathbf{B}^{k-2}-m_{\pm}g\hat{e}_{3})(F^{k-1}_{\pm}-F^{n-1}_{\pm})\pm((\mathbf{E}^{k-2}-\mathbf{E}^{n-2})+\hat{v}_{\pm}\times(\mathbf{B}^{k-2}-\mathbf{B}^{n-2}))F^{n-1}_{\pm}.

We use these two split terms in each of the differences
(\mathbf{E}^{k-1}-\mathbf{E}^{n-1})_{\pm,S}
.
We will use the kernel estimates (
5.29
) and (
5.30
) for the difference
(\mathbf{E}^{k-1}-\mathbf{E}^{n-1})_{\pm,S}
. Then we obtain

\begin{split}&|(\mathbf{E}^{k-1}-\mathbf{E}^{n-1})^{(1)}_{\pm,S}(t,x)|\\
&\lesssim\int_{B^{+}(x;t)}\frac{dy}{|x-y|}\int_{{\mathbb{R}^{3}}}dv\ \frac{v^{0}_{\pm}}{m_{\pm}^{2}}\bigg|(\pm\mathbf{E}^{k-2}\pm\hat{v}_{\pm}\times\mathbf{B}^{k-2}-m_{\pm}g\hat{e}_{3})(F^{k-1}_{\pm}-F^{n-1}_{\pm})(t-|x-y|,y,v)\\
&\pm((\mathbf{E}^{k-2}-\mathbf{E}^{n-2})+\hat{v}_{\pm}\times(\mathbf{B}^{k-2}-\mathbf{B}^{n-2}))F^{n-1}_{\pm}(t-|x-y|,y,v)\bigg|.\end{split}

Here, we again make the changes of variables
y\mapsto y-x=r\omega
with
r\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}|y-x|
and
\omega\in\mathbb{S}^{2}
, and then
r\mapsto\tau\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}t-r
to obtain that

\begin{split}&|(\mathbf{E}^{k-1}-\mathbf{E}^{n-1})^{(1)}_{\pm,S}(t,x)|\\
&\lesssim\frac{C_{m_{\pm},\delta}}{m_{\pm}}\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\ (t-\tau)\bigg[(\|(\mathbf{E}^{k-2}(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{B}^{k-2}(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+m_{\pm}g)\|((v^{0}_{\pm})^{4+\delta}(F^{k-1}_{\pm}-F^{n-1}_{\pm}))(\tau,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\\
&+(\|(\mathbf{E}^{k-2}-\mathbf{E}^{n-2})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|(\mathbf{B}^{k-2}-\mathbf{B}^{n-2})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})\|F^{n-1}_{\pm}(\tau,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\bigg]\\
&\lesssim C_{m_{\pm},\delta}gt\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\ \|((v^{0}_{\pm})^{4+\delta}(F^{k-1}_{\pm}-F^{n-1}_{\pm}))(\tau,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\\
&+\frac{C_{m_{\pm},\delta}t}{m_{\pm}}\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\ (\|(\mathbf{E}^{k-2}-\mathbf{E}^{n-2})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|(\mathbf{B}^{k-2}-\mathbf{B}^{n-2})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}),\end{split}

using the
L^{\infty}
estimates (
5.6
), (
4.18
) and (
5.7
), (
2.11
) for
F^{n-1}_{\pm}
,
\mathbf{E}^{k-2}
,
\mathbf{B}^{k-2}
obtained via the bootstrap arguments. Estimates for
(\mathbf{E}^{k-1}-\mathbf{E}^{n-1})^{(2)}_{\pm,S}
also give the same upper bound. Therefore, we conclude that

\begin{split}|(\mathbf{E}^{k-1}-\mathbf{E}^{n-1})_{\pm,S}(t,x)|&\lesssim C_{m_{\pm},\delta}gt\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\ \|((v^{0}_{\pm})^{4+\delta}(F^{k-1}_{\pm}-F^{n-1}_{\pm}))(\tau,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\\
&+\frac{C_{m_{\pm},\delta}t}{m_{\pm}}\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\ (\|(\mathbf{E}^{k-2}-\mathbf{E}^{n-2})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|(\mathbf{B}^{k-2}-\mathbf{B}^{n-2})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}).\end{split}

Lastly, for the differences in the field components
\mathbf{E}^{k-1}_{3}-\mathbf{E}^{n-1}_{3}
,
\mathbf{B}^{k-1}_{1}-\mathbf{B}^{n-1}_{1}
, and
\mathbf{B}^{k-1}_{2}-\mathbf{B}^{n-1}_{2}
which satisfy the Neumann-type boundary conditions for wave equations, the following additional terms appear in the differences:

I_{1}=2\int_{B(x;t)\cap\{y_{3}=0\}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{(F^{k-1}_{\pm}-F^{n-1}_{\pm})(t-|y-x|,y_{\parallel},0,v)}{|y-x|}dvdS_{y}.

Note that the term
I_{1}
is bounded from above as

(8.9)

I_{1}\lesssim 4\pi C_{m_{\pm},\delta}\int_{0}^{t-x_{3}}d\tau\ \left\|((v^{0}_{\pm})^{4+\delta}(F^{k-1}_{\pm}-F^{n-1}_{\pm}))\left(\tau,\cdot,\cdot\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}},

by the estimate (
8.7
).

Collecting all the estimates for the components of
\mathbf{E}^{k-1}-\mathbf{E}^{n-1}
and
\mathbf{B}^{k-1}-\mathbf{B}^{n-1}
, we conclude that

(8.10)

\begin{split}&|(\mathbf{E}^{k-1}-\mathbf{E}^{n-1})(t,x)|+|(\mathbf{B}^{k-1}-\mathbf{B}^{n-1})(t,x)|\\
&\leq C\bigg((1+t)\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\ \sum_{\pm}\left\|((v^{0}_{\pm})^{4+\delta}(F^{k-1}_{\pm}-F^{n-1}_{\pm}))\left(\tau,\cdot,\cdot\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\\
&+t\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\ (\|(\mathbf{E}^{k-2}-\mathbf{E}^{n-2})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|(\mathbf{B}^{k-2}-\mathbf{B}^{n-2})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})\bigg),\end{split}

where the constant
C
depends only on
m_{\pm},

g
, and
\delta
.
By using (
8.4
) in (
8.10
) we further obtain that

(8.11)

\begin{split}&|(\mathbf{E}^{k-1}-\mathbf{E}^{n-1})(t,x)|+|(\mathbf{B}^{k-1}-\mathbf{B}^{n-1})(t,x)|\\
&\leq C\bigg((1+t)\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\ C_{\tau}^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\tau}ds(\|(\mathbf{E}^{k-2}-\mathbf{E}^{n-2})(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|(\mathbf{B}^{k-2}-\mathbf{B}^{n-2})(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})\\
&+t\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\ (\|(\mathbf{E}^{k-2}-\mathbf{E}^{n-2})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|(\mathbf{B}^{k-2}-\mathbf{B}^{n-2})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})\bigg)\\
&\leq C((1+t)tC_{t}^{\prime}+t)\int_{0}^{t}ds(\|(\mathbf{E}^{k-2}-\mathbf{E}^{n-2})(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|(\mathbf{B}^{k-2}-\mathbf{B}^{n-2})(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}),\end{split}

noting that the coefficient
C^{\prime}_{\tau}
in (
8.4
) has its maximum at
\tau=t
for
\tau\in[0,t].
Now, define
C^{\prime\prime}_{t}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}C((1+t)tC_{t}^{\prime}+t).
By iterating (
8.11
), we finally obtain

(8.12)

\begin{split}&|(\mathbf{E}^{k-1}-\mathbf{E}^{n-1})(t,x)|+|(\mathbf{B}^{k-1}-\mathbf{B}^{n-1})(t,x)|\\
&\leq C^{\prime\prime}_{t}\int_{0}^{t}ds(\|(\mathbf{E}^{k-2}-\mathbf{E}^{n-2})(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|(\mathbf{B}^{k-2}-\mathbf{B}^{n-2})(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})\\
&\leq(C^{\prime\prime}_{t})^{n-1}\int_{0}^{t}ds\ \left(\prod_{j=1}^{n-2}\int_{0}^{\tau_{j-1}}d\tau_{j}\right)\ (\|(\mathbf{E}^{k-n}-\mathbf{E}^{0})(\tau_{n-2},\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|(\mathbf{B}^{k-n}-\mathbf{B}^{0})(\tau_{n-2},\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})\\
&\leq\frac{1}{8}(C^{\prime\prime}_{t})^{n-1}\max\{m_{+},m_{-}\}g\frac{t^{n-1}}{(n-1)!},\end{split}

given that
\mathbf{E}^{0}=\mathbf{B}^{0}=0
and the uniform estimate (
4.18
), (
5.7
), and (
2.11
) for
\mathbf{E}^{k-n}
and
\mathbf{B}^{k-n}
obtained via the bootstrap argument. We also used the notation
\tau_{0}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}s
. Lastly, plugging (
8.12
) into (
8.4
), we obtain for
t\in[0,T]

|((v_{\pm}^{0})^{4+\delta}(F^{k}_{\pm}-F^{n}_{\pm}))(t,x,v)|\leq\frac{1}{8}(C^{\prime\prime}_{t})^{n}\max\{m_{+},m_{-}\}g\frac{t^{n}}{n!},

which can be made sufficiently small as
n
gets sufficiently large. This is via the Stirling approximation that

n!\approx\sqrt{2\pi n}\left(\frac{n}{e}\right)^{n},

and that
C^{\prime\prime}_{t}\leq C^{\prime\prime}_{T}.
This completes the proof of Proposition
8.1
that states
(F^{l}_{\pm}(t,x,v))_{l\in\mathbb{N}}
is Cauchy for each fixed
(t,x,v)
.
∎

Given the Cauchy property of the sequences, we are now ready to pass to the limit. For the linear terms, we directly pass to the limit via the subsequence
l_{k_{i}}
as
k_{i}\to\infty
for each
i=1,2,...,6
by testing with any given
C^{\infty}_{c}
test function which is also a
L^{1}
function.

Also, for the nonlinear terms appearing in (
5.3
) in the case of steady states with Jüttner-Maxwell upper bound in
\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+},
we observe that

(8.13)

\begin{split}&\left|\iiint\phi\left((\mathbf{E}^{l_{k_{6}}}+(\hat{v}_{\pm})\times\mathbf{B}^{l_{k_{6}}})\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{l_{k_{6}}+1}_{\pm}-(\mathbf{E}^{\infty}+(\hat{v}_{\pm})\times\mathbf{B}^{\infty})\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{l_{k_{6}}+1}_{\pm}\right)\right|\\
&=\left|-\iiint\nabla_{v}\phi\cdot\left((\mathbf{E}^{l_{k_{6}}}+(\hat{v}_{\pm})\times\mathbf{B}^{l_{k_{6}}})f^{l_{k_{6}}+1}_{\pm}-(\mathbf{E}^{\infty}+(\hat{v}_{\pm})\times\mathbf{B}^{\infty})f^{\infty}_{\pm}\right)\right|\\
&\leq\left|\iiint\nabla_{v}\phi\cdot(\mathbf{E}^{l_{k_{6}}}+(\hat{v}_{\pm})\times\mathbf{B}^{l_{k_{6}}})(f^{l_{k_{6}}+1}_{\pm}-f^{\infty}_{\pm})\right|+\left|\iiint\nabla_{v}\phi\cdot(\mathbf{E}^{l_{k_{6}}}-\mathbf{E}^{\infty}+(\hat{v}_{\pm})\times\mathbf{B}^{l_{k_{6}}}-(\hat{v}_{\pm})\times\mathbf{B}^{\infty})f^{\infty}_{\pm}\right|\\
&\leq(\|\mathbf{E}^{l_{k_{6}}}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\mathbf{B}^{l_{k_{6}}}\|_{L^{\infty}})\iiint|\nabla_{v}\phi||f^{l_{k_{6}}+1}_{\pm}-f^{\infty}_{\pm}|+\|f^{\infty}_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}}\iiint|\nabla_{v}\phi|\left(|\mathbf{E}^{l_{k_{6}}}-\mathbf{E}^{\infty}|+|\mathbf{B}^{l_{k_{6}}}-\mathbf{B}^{\infty}|\right)\to 0,\end{split}

as
k_{6}\to\infty
for any
C_{c}^{\infty}
test function
\phi,
since
(\mathscr{E}^{l_{k_{6}}},\mathscr{B}^{l_{k_{6}}})
converges strongly as
k_{6}\to\infty
and
f_{\pm}^{l}
converges strongly as
l\to\infty
so that we can use the dominated convergence theorem and the
L^{\infty}
upper-bounds of
\mathbf{E}^{l_{k_{6}}}
and
\mathbf{B}^{l_{k_{6}}}
. Thus, we conclude that
(f^{\infty}_{\pm},\mathscr{E}^{\infty},\mathscr{B}^{\infty})
also solves the original Vlasov–Maxwell system (
1.1
) as the perturbations from the steady states with Jüttner-Maxwell upper bound
(F_{\pm,\textup{st}},\mathbf{E}_{\textup{st}},\mathbf{B}_{\textup{st}})
in the weak sense.

8.2.
Uniqueness and Non-Negativity

We now prove the uniqueness of solutions to the dynamical Vlasov–Maxwell system (
1.1
).
The decay estimate for the momentum derivatives
\nabla_{v}F_{\pm}
(Proposition
6.8
) plays a crucial role in this proof.

Suppose that there are two global-in-time solutions
(F_{\pm}^{(1)},\mathbf{E}^{(1)},\mathbf{B}^{(1)})
and
(F_{\pm}^{(2)},\mathbf{E}^{(2)},\mathbf{B}^{(2)})
for the system (
1.1
) in the time interval
[0,T]
with (
2.13
), (
1.5
), and (
1.4
).Then note that we have

(8.14)

\begin{split}(&F_{\pm}^{(1)}-F_{\pm}^{(2)})(0,x,v)=0,\ (F_{\pm}^{(1)}-F_{\pm}^{(2)})(t,x_{\parallel},0,v)|_{\gamma_{-}}=0,\end{split}

and the difference
F_{\pm}^{(1)}-F_{\pm}^{(2)}
solves the following Vlasov equation:

(8.15)

\partial_{t}(F_{\pm}^{(1)}-F_{\pm}^{(2)})+(\hat{v}_{\pm})\cdot\nabla_{x}(F_{\pm}^{(1)}-F_{\pm}^{(2)})+\left(\pm\mathbf{E}^{(1)}\pm(\hat{v}_{\pm})\times\mathbf{B}^{(1)}-m_{\pm}g\hat{e}_{3}\right)\cdot\nabla_{v}(F_{\pm}^{(1)}-F_{\pm}^{(2)})\\
=-\left(\pm(\mathbf{E}^{(1)}-\mathbf{E}^{(2)})\pm(\hat{v}_{\pm})\times(\mathbf{B}^{(1)}-\mathbf{B}^{(2)})\right)\cdot\nabla_{v}F_{\pm}^{(2)}.

Note that the characteristic trajectory follows the one generated by the fields
\mathbf{E}^{(1)}
and
\mathbf{B}^{(1)}
. Then, by integrating (
8.15
) along the characteristics
\mathscr{Z}_{\pm}(s)=(\mathscr{X}_{\pm}(s),\mathscr{V}_{\pm}(s))
(associated with
\mathbf{E}^{(1)}
and
\mathbf{B}^{(1)}
) for
s\in[\max\{0,t-t_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}\},t]
defined in the sense of (
3.38
), we obtain

(8.16)

(F_{\pm}^{(1)}-F_{\pm}^{(2)})(t,x,v)=\mp\int^{t}_{\max\{0,t-t_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}\}}\left((\mathbf{E}^{(1)}-\mathbf{E}^{(2)})(s,\mathscr{X}_{\pm}(s))+\hat{\mathscr{V}}_{\pm}(s)\times(\mathbf{B}^{(1)}-\mathbf{B}^{(2)})(s,\mathscr{X}_{\pm}(s))\right)\\
\cdot\nabla_{v}F_{\pm}^{(2)}(s,\mathscr{X}_{\pm}(s),\mathscr{V}_{\pm}(s))ds.

Therefore, we obtain

(8.17)

|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{4+\delta}(F_{\pm}^{(1)}-F_{\pm}^{(2)}))(t,x,v)|\\
\leq C_{T}\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\|((v^{0}_{\pm})^{4+\delta}\nabla_{v}F_{\pm}^{(2)})(s,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\int_{0}^{t}\left(\|(\mathbf{E}^{(1)}-\mathbf{E}^{(2)})(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|(\mathbf{B}^{(1)}-\mathbf{B}^{(2)})(s,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\right)ds,

by the energy comparison (
8.3
). Now we make upper bound estimates on the
\mathbf{E}^{(1)}-\mathbf{E}^{(2)}
and
\mathbf{B}^{(1)}-\mathbf{B}^{(2)}
differences using the representations (
A.1
), (
A.4
), (
3.33
), and (
3.37
). Note that
(\mathbf{E}^{(1)},\mathbf{B}^{(1)})
and
(\mathbf{E}^{(2)},\mathbf{B}^{(2)})
satisfy the same initial-boundary data, and hence their difference have zero homogeneous terms in their representations. For the rest of the terms including
b2,b1,T,S
terms, we follow the exactly same argument from (
8.5
) to (
8.9
) with
F_{\pm}^{(1)}=F^{k-1}_{\pm}
,
F_{\pm}^{(2)}=F^{n-1}_{\pm}
,
\mathbf{E}^{(1)}=\mathbf{E}^{k-1}=\mathbf{E}^{k-2}
,

\mathbf{E}^{(2)}=\mathbf{E}^{n-1}=\mathbf{E}^{n-2}
,

\mathbf{B}^{(1)}=\mathbf{B}^{k-1}=\mathbf{B}^{k-2}
, and

\mathbf{B}^{(2)}=\mathbf{B}^{n-1}=\mathbf{B}^{n-2}
, we obtain

|(\mathbf{E}^{(1)}-\mathbf{B}^{(2)})(t,x)|+|(\mathbf{B}^{(1)}-\mathbf{B}^{(2)})(t,x)|\leq C\bigg((1+t)\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\ \sum_{\iota=\pm}\left\|((v^{0}_{\iota})^{4+\delta}(F_{\iota}^{(1)}-F_{\iota}^{(2)}))\left(\tau,\cdot,\cdot\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\\
+t\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\ (\|(\mathbf{E}^{(1)}-\mathbf{E}^{(2)})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|(\mathbf{B}^{(1)}-\mathbf{B}^{(2)})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})\bigg),

by (
8.10
). Then by the Grönwall lemma, we obtain for
t\in[0,T]
,

(8.18)

\|(\mathbf{E}^{(1)}-\mathbf{E}^{(2)})(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|(\mathbf{B}^{(1)}-\mathbf{B}^{(2)})(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq C\bigg((1+t)\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\ \sum_{\iota=\pm}\left\|((v^{0}_{\iota})^{4+\delta}(F_{\iota}^{(1)}-F_{\iota}^{(2)}))\left(\tau,\cdot,\cdot\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\bigg)e^{\frac{t^{2}}{2}}.

Plugging (
8.18
) into (
8.17
), we obtain

\begin{split}|(v^{0}_{\pm})^{4+\delta}(F_{\pm}^{(1)}-F_{\pm}^{(2)}))(t,x,v)|&\leq CC_{T}\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\|((v^{0}_{\pm})^{4+\delta}\nabla_{v}F_{\pm}^{(2)})(s,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\\
&\times\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\left((1+\tau)e^{\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}}\int_{0}^{\tau}d\tau^{\prime}\ \sum_{\iota=\pm}\left\|((v^{0}_{\iota})^{4+\delta}(F_{\iota}^{(1)}-F_{\iota}^{(2)}))\left(\tau^{\prime},\cdot,\cdot\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\right)\\
&\leq CC_{T}T(1+T)e^{\frac{T^{2}}{2}}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|((v^{0}_{\pm})^{4+\delta}\nabla_{v}F_{\pm}^{(2)})(s,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\\
&\times\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\left(\sum_{\iota=\pm}\sup_{0\leq\tau^{\prime}\leq\tau}\left\|((v^{0}_{\iota})^{4+\delta}(F_{\iota}^{(1)}-F_{\iota}^{(2)}))\left(\tau^{\prime},\cdot,\cdot\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\right).\end{split}

By defining

\begin{split}D_{T}&\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}CC_{T}T(1+T)e^{\frac{T^{2}}{2}}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|((v^{0}_{\pm})^{4+\delta}\nabla_{v}F_{\pm}^{(2)})(s,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\\
\tilde{U}(\tau)&\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}\sum_{\iota=\pm}\sup_{0\leq\tau^{\prime}\leq\tau}\left\|((v^{0}_{\iota})^{4+\delta}(F_{\iota}^{(1)}-F_{\iota}^{(2)}))\left(\tau^{\prime},\cdot,\cdot\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}},\end{split}

we obtain the Volterra inequality

\tilde{U}(t)\leq 2D_{T}\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{U}(\tau)d\tau.

Further define
U(t)=e^{-2D_{T}t}\tilde{U}(t).
Then we observe that

\frac{d}{dt}U(t)=e^{-2D_{T}t}\frac{d}{dt}\tilde{U}(t)-2D_{T}\tilde{U}(t)\leq 0.

Therefore,
U(t)
is non-decreasing. Since
U(0)=0
by
(F_{\pm}^{(1)}-F_{\pm}^{(2)})(0,\cdot,\cdot)=0,
we have that
U(t)=0
for any
t\geq 0
since
U(t)
is non-negative.
Therefore, we conclude that

\sum_{\iota=\pm}\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq t}\left\|((v^{0}_{\iota})^{4+\delta}(F_{\iota}^{(1)}-F_{\iota}^{(2)}))\left(\tau,\cdot,\cdot\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}=0.

Then this also implies that
\mathbf{E}^{(1)}=\mathbf{E}^{(2)}
and
\mathbf{B}^{(1)}=\mathbf{B}^{(2)}
almost everywhere by (
8.18
). This completes the proof of the uniqueness.

Lastly, for the proof of non-negativity, assume that the initial distributions
F_{\pm}^{\textup{in}}
and the inflow boundary profile
G_{\pm}
are non-negative. Since
F_{\pm}
remains constant along the characteristics described by (
3.38
), it follows that
F_{\pm}
is also non-negative.

Consequently, Proposition
5.2
, Proposition
6.8
, Proposition
6.9
, Proposition
7.1
and Proposition
8.1
together with the uniqueness and the non-negativity completes the proof of our main well-posedness theorem (Theorem
2.3
) of the paper. In the next section, we lastly provide a generalized setting for astrophysical applications.

9.
Discussion on Astrophysical Applications

Many astrophysical environments, such as the regions surrounding stars, can be modeled using the Vlasov–Maxwell system, which describes the interaction of charged particles with electromagnetic fields.
Consider, for example, a star and near the star, intense gravitational and electromagnetic forces dominate, making the Vlasov–Maxwell system under a constant gravitational field a relevant model (see
[
22
]
).

In this generalized model, the Lorentz force term in (
1.1
) becomes

\pm\left(\mathbf{E}+\mathbf{E}_{\textup{ext}}+\hat{v}_{\pm}\times(\mathbf{B}+\mathbf{B}_{\textup{ext}})\mp m_{\pm}g\hat{e}_{3}\right),

where
(\mathbf{E}_{\textup{ext}},\mathbf{B}_{\textup{ext}})
are fixed, time-independent background fields.
To preserve the gravitational confinement mechanism, we assume the physically natural smallness conditions

(9.1)

|\mathbf{E}_{\textup{ext},3}|\ll\min\{m_{-},m_{+}\}g,\quad|\mathbf{B}_{\textup{ext},1}|,\,|\mathbf{B}_{\textup{ext},2}|\ll\min\{m_{-},m_{+}\}g.

The condition on
\mathbf{E}_{\textup{ext},3}
is well-known in plasma physics, as it ensures that the net vertical force remains directed inward toward the boundary, preserving particle confinement. The assumptions on the transverse magnetic components
\mathbf{B}_{\textup{ext},1},\mathbf{B}_{\textup{ext},2}
are imposed to control the additional drift effects introduced by the magnetic force
\hat{v}\times\mathbf{B}_{\textup{ext}}
, which otherwise may dominate the stabilizing gravitational force.

Given these assumptions, the only part of the nonlinear analysis that requires modification is the estimate on the backward exit time
t_{\mathbf{b}}(t,x,v)
. The original estimate (
3.56
) in the gravitational-only setting must be adapted to account for the influence of the external fields. In particular, under the smallness conditions above, we can prove that the modified backward exit time still satisfies a comparable upper bound:

Lemma 9.1
.

Suppose (
9.1
) holds. Then the backward exit time
t_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}
(Definition
3.9
) satisfies

(9.2)

t_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}(t,x,v)\leq\frac{C}{m_{\pm}g}(v^{0}_{\pm}+m_{\pm}gx_{3}),

with a constant
C>0
that depends on the relative magnitudes of
\mathbf{E}_{\textup{ext},3}
and
\mathbf{B}_{\textup{ext},\parallel}
.

The remainder of the proof structure, including all the decay estimates and the nonlinear bootstrap, remains unchanged.

In the non-relativistic setting, this estimate can be verified more explicitly by Taylor-expanding the vertical trajectory under the total force and observing that the dominant term is still governed by gravity when
|\mathbf{B}_{\textup{ext},\parallel}|\ll\min\{m_{-},m_{+}\}g
or the associated Larmor frequency. For the relativistic case, a fully explicit formula for the exit time may not be available. However, as shown in
[
22
]
, a similar conclusion holds under analogous smallness assumptions on the external field components.

We therefore conclude that our results naturally extend to the more general setting with fixed ambient fields
(\mathbf{E}_{\textup{ext}},\mathbf{B}_{\textup{ext}})
, provided the vertical component of the external electric field and the horizontal components of the magnetic field are small in comparison to the gravitational force. The initial theorems stated in Sections
1
and 2 can accordingly be reformulated for the full system. All later sections (Sections
3
through
8
) remain valid as written, except for the single modification to the exit time estimate.

We close this section by introducing a generalized weight comparison argument to derive the upper bound estimates (
9.2
) on the backward exit time
t_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}
.

Proof of Lemma
9.1
.

Suppose that the self-consistent electromagnetic fields
(\mathbf{E},\mathbf{B})
satisfies the following assumption:

(9.3)

\sup_{t}\|(\mathbf{E},\mathbf{B})\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\min\{m_{+},m_{-}\}\frac{g}{16}.

Also, in (
9.1
), we suppose the following assumption on the external background fields
(\mathbf{E}_{\textup{ext}},\mathbf{B}_{\textup{ext}})
:

(9.4)

|\mathbf{E}_{\textup{ext},3}|\leq\min\{m_{+},m_{-}\}\frac{g}{16},\quad|\mathbf{B}_{\textup{ext},1}|,\,|\mathbf{B}_{\textup{ext},2}|\leq\min\{m_{+},m_{-}\}\frac{g}{16}.

Define the characteristic trajectory
(\mathscr{X}_{\pm},\mathscr{V}_{\pm})
such that now we have

\frac{d\mathscr{V}_{\pm}}{ds}=\pm(\mathbf{E}+\mathbf{E}_{\text{ext}}\pm\hat{\mathscr{V}_{\pm}}\times(\mathbf{B}+\mathbf{B}_{\text{ext}}))-m_{\pm}g\hat{e}_{3}.

Then we have

(9.5)

\frac{d}{ds}\left(\sqrt{m_{\pm}^{2}+|\mathscr{V}_{\pm}(s)|^{2}}+m_{\pm}g(\mathscr{X}_{\pm})_{3}(s)\right)=\hat{\mathscr{V}_{\pm}}(s)\cdot\frac{d\mathscr{V}_{\pm}}{ds}+m_{\pm}g(\hat{\mathscr{V}_{\pm}})_{3}(s)\\
=\pm\hat{\mathscr{V}_{\pm}}(s)\cdot(\mathbf{E}(s,\mathscr{X}_{\pm}(s))+\mathbf{E}_{\text{ext}}(s,\mathscr{X}_{\pm}(s))).

Then we observe that by (
9.3
) and (
9.4
), we have

\frac{d(\mathscr{V}_{\pm})_{3}}{ds}(s)=-(\mathbf{E}+\mathbf{E}_{\textup{ext}}+\hat{\mathscr{V}_{\pm}}\times(\mathbf{B}+\mathbf{B}_{\textup{ext}}))_{3}-m_{\pm}g\\
=\mathbf{E}_{3}+\mathbf{E}_{\textup{ext},3}+(\hat{\mathscr{V}_{\pm}})_{1}(\mathbf{B}_{2}+\mathbf{B}_{\textup{ext},2})-(\hat{\mathscr{V}_{\pm}})_{2}(\mathbf{B}_{1}+\mathbf{B}_{\textup{ext},1})-m_{\pm}g\leq-\frac{3}{4}m_{\pm}g,

since
|\hat{\mathscr{V}_{\pm}}|\leq 1.

Now if we define a trajectory variable
s^{*}=s^{*}(t,x,v)\in[t-t_{\pm,\mathbf{b}},t+t_{\pm,\mathbf{f}}]
such that
(\mathscr{V}_{\pm})_{3}(s^{*};t,x,v)=0
, then we have

\displaystyle(\mathscr{V}_{\pm})_{3}(t+t_{\pm,\mathbf{f}})-(\mathscr{V}_{\pm})_{3}(s^{*})

\displaystyle=\int^{t+t_{\pm,\mathbf{f}}}_{s^{*}}\frac{d(\mathscr{V}_{\pm})_{3}}{ds}(\tau)d\tau\leq-\frac{3}{4}m_{\pm}g(t+t_{\pm,\mathbf{f}}-s^{*}),\text{ and }

\displaystyle(\mathscr{V}_{\pm})_{3}(s^{*})-(\mathscr{V}_{\pm})_{3}(t-t_{\pm,\mathbf{b}})

\displaystyle=\int_{t-t_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}}^{s^{*}}\frac{d(\mathscr{V}_{\pm})_{3}}{ds}(\tau)d\tau\leq-\frac{3}{4}m_{\pm}g(s^{*}-(t-t_{\pm,\mathbf{b}})).

Therefore, we have

(9.6)

t_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}+t_{\pm,\mathbf{f}}\leq-\frac{4}{3m_{\pm}g}((\mathscr{V}_{\pm})_{3}(t+t_{\pm,\mathbf{f}})-(\mathscr{V}_{\pm})_{3}(t-t_{\pm,\mathbf{b}})).

On the other hand, using (
9.3
)-(
9.5
), we have

\sqrt{m_{\pm}^{2}+|\mathscr{V}_{\pm}(t-t_{\pm,\mathbf{b}})|^{2}}=\left(v^{0}_{\pm}+m_{\pm}gx_{3}\right)\pm\int_{t}^{t-t_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}}\hat{\mathscr{V}_{\pm}}(s)\cdot(\mathbf{E}(s,\mathscr{X}_{\pm}(s))+\mathbf{E}_{\text{ext}}(s,\mathscr{X}_{\pm}(s)))ds\\
\leq\left(v^{0}_{\pm}+m_{\pm}gx_{3}\right)+\frac{m_{\pm}g}{8}t_{\pm,\mathbf{b}},

and

\sqrt{m_{\pm}^{2}+|\mathscr{V}_{\pm}(t+t_{\pm,\mathbf{f}})|^{2}}=\left(v^{0}_{\pm}+m_{\pm}gx_{3}\right)\pm\int_{t}^{t+t_{\pm,\mathbf{f}}}\hat{\mathscr{V}_{\pm}}(s)\cdot(\mathbf{E}(s,\mathscr{X}_{\pm}(s))+\mathbf{E}_{\text{ext}}(s,\mathscr{X}_{\pm}(s)))ds\\
\leq\left(v^{0}_{\pm}+m_{\pm}gx_{3}\right)+\frac{m_{\pm}g}{8}t_{\pm,\mathbf{f}}.

Thus, together with (
9.6
), we have

t_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}+t_{\pm,\mathbf{f}}\leq\frac{4}{3m_{\pm}g}\left(2(v^{0}_{\pm}+m_{\pm}gx_{3})+\frac{m_{\pm}g}{8}(t_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}+t_{\pm,\mathbf{f}})\right).

Therefore, we have

t_{\pm,\mathbf{b}}+t_{\pm,\mathbf{f}}\leq\frac{16}{5m_{\pm}g}(v^{0}_{\pm}+m_{\pm}gx_{3}),

and this completes the proof of (
9.2
).
∎

Appendix A
Electric Field Representation

For the representation of the self-consistent electric field
\mathbf{E}
in the half space
\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}
, we follow the half-space Glassey-Strauss formula derived in
[
4
, eq. (35), (37)-(41), (47)-(50)]
as follows. We write
\mathbf{E}=\mathbf{E}_{\textup{hom}}+\mathbf{E}_{\textup{par}}
where the tangential components
\mathbf{E}_{\textup{par},\parallel}
of the particular solution (
i=1,2
) are given by

(A.1)

\begin{split}\mathbf{E}_{\textup{par},i}(t,x)=&\sum_{\iota=\pm}(-\iota)\int_{B^{+}(x;t)}dy\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dv\ a^{\mathbf{E}}_{\iota,i}(v,\omega)\cdot(\iota\mathbf{E}+\iota(\hat{v}_{\iota})\times\mathbf{B}-m_{\iota}g\hat{e}_{3})\frac{F_{\iota}(t-|x-y|,y,v)}{|x-y|}\\
&+\sum_{\iota=\pm}\iota\int_{\partial B(x;t)\cap\{y_{3}>0\}}\frac{dS_{y}}{|y-x|}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dv\ \left(\delta_{ij}-\frac{(\omega_{i}+(\hat{v}_{\iota})_{i})(\hat{v}_{\iota})_{j}}{1+\hat{v}_{\iota}\cdot\omega}\right)\omega^{j}F_{\iota}(0,y,v)\\
&+\sum_{\iota=\pm}(-\iota)\int_{B(x;t)\cap\{y_{3}=0\}}\frac{dy_{\parallel}}{|y-x|}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dv\left(\delta_{i3}-\frac{(\omega_{i}+(\hat{v}_{\iota})_{i})(\hat{v}_{\iota})_{3}}{1+\hat{v}_{\iota}\cdot\omega}\right)F_{\iota}(t-|x-y|,y_{\parallel},0,v)\\
&+\sum_{\iota=\pm}(-\iota)\int_{B^{+}(x;t)}\frac{dy}{|y-x|^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dv\ \frac{(|(\hat{v}_{\iota})|^{2}-1)((\hat{v}_{\iota})_{i}+\omega_{i})}{(1+\hat{v}_{\iota}\cdot\omega)^{2}}F_{\iota}(t-|x-y|,y,v)\\
&+\sum_{\iota=\pm}\iota\int_{B^{-}(x;t)}dy\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dv\ a^{\mathbf{E}}_{\iota,i}(v,\bar{\omega})\cdot(\iota\mathbf{E}+\iota(\hat{v}_{\iota})\times\mathbf{B}-m_{\iota}g\hat{e}_{3})\frac{F_{\iota}(t-|x-y|,\bar{y},v)}{|x-y|}\\
&+\sum_{\iota=\pm}(-\iota)\int_{\partial B(x;t)\cap\{y_{3}<0\}}\frac{dS_{y}}{|y-x|}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dv\ (\delta_{ij}-\frac{(\bar{\omega}_{i}+(\hat{v}_{\iota})_{i})(\hat{v}_{\iota})_{j}}{1+(\hat{v}_{\iota})\cdot\bar{\omega}})\bar{\omega}^{j}F_{\iota}(0,\bar{y},v)\\
&+\sum_{\iota=\pm}\iota\int_{B(x;t)\cap\{y_{3}=0\}}\frac{dy_{\parallel}}{|y-x|}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dv\ (\delta_{i3}-\frac{(\bar{\omega}_{i}+(\hat{v}_{\iota})_{i})(\hat{v}_{\iota})_{3}}{1+(\hat{v}_{\iota})\cdot\bar{\omega}})F_{\iota}(t-|x-y|,y_{\parallel},0,v)\\
&+\sum_{\iota=\pm}\iota\int_{B^{-}(x;t)}\frac{dy}{|y-x|^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dv\ \frac{(|(\hat{v}_{\iota})|^{2}-1)((\hat{v}_{\iota})_{i}+\bar{\omega}_{i})}{(1+(\hat{v}_{\iota})\cdot\bar{\omega})^{2}}F_{\iota}(t-|x-y|,\bar{y},v)\\
\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}&\sum_{\iota=\pm}\iota(\mathbf{E}^{(1)}_{\iota,iS}+\mathbf{E}^{(1)}_{\iota,ib1}+\mathbf{E}^{(1)}_{\iota,ib2}+\mathbf{E}^{(1)}_{\iota,iT}-\mathbf{E}^{(2)}_{\iota,iS}-\mathbf{E}^{(2)}_{\iota,ib1}-\mathbf{E}^{(2)}_{\iota,ib2}-\mathbf{E}^{(2)}_{\iota,iT}),\end{split}

where
\bar{z}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}(z_{1},z_{2},-z_{3})^{\top}
,
B^{\pm}(x;t)
are the upper- and the lower open half balls, respectively, defined as
B^{+}(x;t)=B(x;t)\cap\{y_{3}>0\}
and
B^{-}(x;t)=B(x;t)\cap\{y_{3}<0\}
,
dy_{\parallel}
is the 2-dimensional Lebesgue measure on
B(x;t)\cap\{y_{3}=0\}
, and

(A.2)

a^{\mathbf{E}}_{\iota,i}(v,\omega)\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}\nabla_{v}\left(\frac{\omega_{i}+(\hat{v}_{\iota})_{i}}{1+\hat{v}_{\iota}\cdot\omega}\right)=\frac{(\partial_{v_{i}}v-(\hat{v}_{\iota})_{i}(\hat{v}_{\iota}))(1+\hat{v}_{\iota}\cdot\omega)-(\omega_{i}+(\hat{v}_{\iota})_{i})(\omega-(\omega\cdot(\hat{v}_{\iota}))(\hat{v}_{\iota}))}{(v^{0}_{\iota})(1+\hat{v}_{\iota}\cdot\omega)^{2}}.

For the tangential component
\mathbf{E}_{\textup{hom},\parallel}
of the homogeneous solution, we have

(A.3)

\mathbf{E}_{\textup{hom},i}(t,x)=\frac{1}{4\pi t^{2}}\int_{\partial B(x;t)\cap\{y_{3}>0\}}\left(t\mathbf{E}^{1}_{0i}(y)+\mathbf{E}_{0i}(y)+\nabla\mathbf{E}_{0i}(y)\cdot(y-x)\right)dS_{y}\\
-\frac{1}{4\pi t^{2}}\int_{\partial B(x;t)\cap\{y_{3}<0\}}\left(t\mathbf{E}^{1}_{0i}(\bar{y})+\mathbf{E}_{0i}(\bar{y})+\nabla\mathbf{E}_{0i}(\bar{y})\cdot(\bar{y}-\bar{x})\right)dS_{y},

where
\bar{z}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}(z_{1},z_{2},-z_{3})^{\top}
.

On the other hand, for the normal component
\mathbf{E}_{3}
, for each
(t,x)\in[0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(0,\infty),
we have the Glassey-Strauss representation as

(A.4)

\begin{split}\mathbf{E}_{3}(t,x)=&\frac{1}{4\pi t^{2}}\int_{\partial B(x;t)\cap\{y_{3}>0\}}\left(t\mathbf{E}^{1}_{03}(y)+\mathbf{E}_{03}(y)+\nabla\mathbf{E}_{03}(y)\cdot(y-x)\right)dS_{y}\\
&+\frac{1}{4\pi t^{2}}\int_{\partial B(x;t)\cap\{y_{3}<0\}}\left(t\mathbf{E}^{1}_{03}(\bar{y})+\mathbf{E}_{03}(\bar{y})+\nabla\mathbf{E}_{03}(\bar{y})\cdot(\bar{y}-\bar{x})\right)dS_{y}\\
&+\sum_{\iota=\pm}\iota\int_{B^{+}(x;t)}dy\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dv\ a^{\mathbf{E}}_{\iota,3}(v,\omega)\cdot(\iota\mathbf{E}+\iota(\hat{v}_{\iota})\times\mathbf{B}-m_{\iota}g\hat{e}_{3})\frac{F_{\iota}(t-|x-y|,y,v)}{|x-y|}\\
&+\sum_{\iota=\pm}(-\iota)\int_{\partial B(x;t)\cap\{y_{3}>0\}}\frac{dS_{y}}{|y-x|}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dv\ \left(\delta_{3j}-\frac{(\omega_{3}+(\hat{v}_{\iota})_{3})(\hat{v}_{\iota})_{j}}{1+\hat{v}_{\iota}\cdot\omega}\right)\omega^{j}F_{\iota}(0,y,v)\\
&+\sum_{\iota=\pm}\iota\int_{B(x;t)\cap\{y_{3}=0\}}\frac{dy_{\parallel}}{|y-x|}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dv\ \left(1-\frac{(\omega_{3}+(\hat{v}_{\iota})_{3})(\hat{v}_{\iota})_{3}}{1+\hat{v}_{\iota}\cdot\omega}\right)F_{\iota}(t-|x-y|,y_{\parallel},0,v)\\
&+\sum_{\iota=\pm}\iota\int_{B^{+}(x;t)}\frac{dy}{|y-x|^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dv\ \frac{(|(\hat{v}_{\iota})|^{2}-1)((\hat{v}_{\iota})_{3}+\omega_{3})}{(1+\hat{v}_{\iota}\cdot\omega)^{2}}F_{\iota}(t-|x-y|,y,v)\\
&+\sum_{\iota=\pm}\iota\int_{B^{-}(x;t)}dy\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dv\ a^{\mathbf{E}}_{\iota,3}(v,\bar{\omega})\cdot(\iota\mathbf{E}+\iota(\hat{v}_{\iota})\times\mathbf{B}-m_{\iota}g\hat{e}_{3})\frac{F_{\iota}(t-|x-y|,\bar{y},v)}{|x-y|}\\
&+\sum_{\iota=\pm}(-\iota)\int_{\partial B(x;t)\cap\{y_{3}<0\}}\frac{dS_{y}}{|y-x|}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dv\ (\delta_{3j}-\frac{(\bar{w}_{3}+(\hat{v}_{\iota})_{3})(\hat{v}_{\iota})_{j}}{1+(\hat{v}_{\iota})\cdot\bar{\omega}})\bar{\omega}^{j}F_{\iota}(0,\bar{y},v)\\
&+\sum_{\iota=\pm}\iota\int_{B(x;t)\cap\{y_{3}=0\}}\frac{dy_{\parallel}}{|y-x|}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dv\ (1-\frac{(\bar{w}_{3}+(\hat{v}_{\iota})_{3})(\hat{v}_{\iota})_{3}}{1+(\hat{v}_{\iota})\cdot\bar{\omega}})F_{\iota}(t-|x-y|,y_{\parallel},0,v)\\
&+\sum_{\iota=\pm}\iota\int_{B^{-}(x;t)}\frac{dy}{|y-x|^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dv\ \frac{(|(\hat{v}_{\iota})|^{2}-1)((\hat{v}_{\iota})_{3}+\bar{w}_{3})}{(1+(\hat{v}_{\iota})\cdot\bar{\omega})^{2}}F_{\iota}(t-|x-y|,\bar{y},v)\\
&+\sum_{\iota=\pm}(-\iota)2\int_{B(x;t)\cap\{y_{3}=0\}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{F_{\iota}(t-|y-x|,y_{\parallel},0,v)}{|y-x|}dvdy_{\parallel},\end{split}

where
a^{\mathbf{E}}_{\iota,3}
is defined in (
A.2
),
\bar{z}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{def}}}{=}(z_{1},z_{2},-z_{3})^{\top}
, and

dy_{\parallel}
is the 2-dimensional Lebesgue measure on
B(x;t)\cap\{y_{3}=0\}
.
